# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2012-10-22_TotalExtraction_34991159.pdf
1
ORDINANCE NO. _____________
ENTITLED:
“AN ORDINANCE CREATING SECTION 6.08.180, FEEDING GEESE AND
DUCKS PROHIBITED, OF CHAPTER 6.08, ANIMAL CARE AND CONTROL
REGULATIONS GENERALLY, OF TITLE 6, ANIMALS, OF THE CODE OF
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE,
WYOMING:
Section 1.  That Section 6.08.180, Feeding geese and ducks prohibited, of Chapter 6.08,
Animal Care and Control Regulations Generally, of Title 6, Animals, of the Code of the City of
Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby created to read as follows:
6.08.180
FEEDING GEESE AND DUCKS PROHIBITED.
A.
NO PERSON MAY FEED, PROVIDE, OR GIVE ANY FOOD OR OTHER EDIBLE
ITEM TO ANY GOOSE OR DUCK, OR TO ANY FLOCK OF GEESE OR DUCKS, IN OR
UPON:
1.
ANY PROPERTY OWNED BY THE CITY OF CHEYENNE WITHIN THE
CITY LIMITS;
2.
ANY PARK, GOLF COURSE, OR OTHER RECREATION, OPEN SPACE,
OR PUBLIC USE AREA WITHIN THE CITY LIMITS WHICH IS OPERATED BY THE
CITY OF CHEYENNE; OR
3.
ANY PARK, GOLF COURSE, OR OTHER RECREATION, OPEN SPACE,
OR PUBLIC USE AREA WITHIN THE CITY LIMITS WHICH IS OWNED BY THE
CITY OF CHEYENNE AND OPERATED BY ANY THIRD PERSON OR ENTITY
PURSUANT TO A LEASE OR OTHER USE AGREEMENT WITH THE CITY OF
CHEYENNE.
B.
ANY PERSON WHO VIOLATES THE PROVISIONS OF THIS SECTION SHALL,
UPON CONVICTION, PAY A FINE OF NOT LESS THAN TEN DOLLARS ($10.00) AND NOT
MORE THAN TWENTY-FIVE DOLLARS ($25.00), TOGETHER WITH COURT COSTS AS
MAY BE ASSESSED BY THE MUNICIPAL COURT FOR CONVICTIONS OF OTHER
VIOLATIONS OF THE CHEYENNE CITY CODE FOR WHICH A CRIMINAL PENALTY IS
PROVIDED.
C.
IN LIEU OF A CITATION, A POLICE OFFICER MAY ISSUE ONE WRITTEN
WARNING TO ANY PERSON WHO HAS NOT PREVIOUSLY BEEN CONVICTED OF
VIOLATING THIS SECTION.
D.
NO MORE THAN ONE CITATION ALLEGING A VIOLATION OF THIS
SECTION MAY BE ISSUED TO ANY ONE PERSON IN ANY TWENTY-FOUR (24) HOUR
PERIOD.

2
Section 2.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.
FIRST READING:______________________________________________________________
SECOND READING:___________________________________________________________
THIRD AND FINAL READING:__________________________________________________
__________________________________________
Richard L. Kaysen, Mayor
(SEAL)
ATTEST:
____________________________________
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk
Publish:  Wyoming Tribune Eagle


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2012-10-22_TotalExtraction_91801469.pdf
 
 
 
 
 
 
RESOLUTION NO________ 
 
 
ENTITLED:  “A RESOLUTION CERTIFYING THAT THE GOVERNING BODY OF 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE IS IN SUPPORT OF THE TOWN OF ALBIN’S PROJECTS 
TO BE SUBMITTED TO THE STATE LOAN AND INVESTMENT BOARD FOR THE 
DECEMBER 2012 COUNTYWIDE CONSENSUS BLOCK GRANT FUNDING IN THE 
TOTAL AMOUNT OF $115,000.” 
 
 
Whereas, the rules of the State Loan and Investment Board require that application for funds 
under the Countywide Consensus Block Grant Program be made by the Board of County 
Commissioners and agreed to by municipalities representing at least 70% of the county’s 
incorporated population; and 
 
Whereas, the City of Cheyenne represents greater than 70% of the incorporated population 
of Laramie County. 
 
THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING  
 
That the City of Cheyenne supports the Town of Albin’s Consensus grant request to the State 
Loan and Investment Board for the projects set forth in the FY 2013/2014 Countywide 
Consensus Block Grant Application Form; and 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS 22nd DAY OF October, 2012. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
                                                                  Richard L. Kaysen, Mayor 
 
ATTEST: 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Carol Inlekofer, City Clerk 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Consensu 12AlbinRes.docx 










--- END OF FILE ---

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2012-10-22_TotalExtraction_82171899.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. ___________
ENTITLED:
“A 
RESOLUTION 
DESIGNATING 
THE 
CHEYENNE 
DOWNTOWN
DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY (“DDA”) AS THE LOCAL MAIN STREET
ORGANIZATION FOR THE IMPLEMENTATION OF THE WYOMING MAIN
STREET PROGRAM; AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR AND CITY CLERK TO
JOIN WITH THE DDA IN THE SUBMISSION OF A WYOMING MAIN
STREET–CERTIFIED TRACK APPLICATION TO THE WYOMING BUSINESS
COUNCIL; AND DESIGNATING THE EXECUTIVE DIRECTOR OF THE DDA
AS THE PROGRAM DIRECTOR FOR THE WYOMING MAIN STREET
PROGRAM IN THE CITY OF CHEYENNE.”
WHEREAS, the Wyoming Main Street program has been created by the Wyoming Business
Council to assist communities in the development of public-private partnerships designed to
revitalize downtown “Main Street” areas; and
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne and the DDA wish to pursue designation as a Certified
Wyoming Main Street Program which requires that the City and the DDA meet certain criteria
established by the National Trust for Historic Preservation Main Street Center (NTMSC) and the
Wyoming Business Council as set forth in the Wyoming Main Street program guidelines attached
hereto as Exhibit A; and
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne and the DDA desire to submit a joint Wyoming Main
Street Certified Track Application to the Wyoming Business Council in the form attached to this
Resolution as Exhibit B.  
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the City of Cheyenne:
1.
Authorizes the Mayor and the City Clerk to join with the DDA in the submission of
a Wyoming Main Street Certified Track Application to the Wyoming Business Council and to
execute such other and further documents as may be necessary to complete the application; 
2.
Designates the DDA as the local Main Street organization which shall be responsible
for the implementation of Wyoming Main Street goals and objectives; 
3.
Designates the Executive Director of the DDA as the local Program Director for the
Wyoming Main Street Program in the City of Cheyenne; 
4.
Agrees to participate in the financial support of the DDA through tax increment
financing currently being provided to the DDA pursuant to W.S. § 15-9-207(c). 
5.
Endorses the goal of economic revitalization of those portions of downtown
Cheyenne which are within the DDA District through the preservation and rehabilitation of its
historic buildings and through such other and further measures as determined to be appropriate by
the Wyoming Main Street Program. 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _________________, 2012.
__________________________________________
Richard L. Kaysen, Mayor
(SEAL)
Attest:
____________________________________
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk


--- END OF FILE ---

RESOLUTION 
 
 
 
 
 
 
ENTITLED:  “A RESOLUTION CERTIFYING THAT THE GOVERNING BODY OF 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE HAS AGREED ON THE TOWN OF BURNS 
SUBSTITUTION PROJECT TO BE SUBMITTED TO THE STATE LOAN AND 
INVESTMENT BOARD FOR THE 2011-2012 COUNTYWIDE CONSENSUS BLOCK 
GRANT FUNDING.” 
 
Whereas, the Governing Bodies of Laramie County and the City of Cheyenne desire to 
participate in the State Loan and Investment Board’s Chapter 32 Countywide Consensus Block 
Grant program; and 
 
Whereas, the rules of the State Loan and Investment Board require that application for funds 
under this program be made by the Board of County Commissioners and agreed to by 
municipalities representing at least 70% of the county’s incorporated population; and 
 
Whereas, the Governing Body for Laramie County and Cheyenne represent greater than 
70% of the incorporated population of Laramie County; 
 
THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING  
 
That the City of Cheyenne support an application from the town of Burns in the amount of 
$23,299.86, through Laramie County,  to the  State Loan and Investment Board for the project 
set forth in the 2011-2012 Countywide Consensus Block Grant Application Form; and 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS 12TH DAY OF NOVEMBER, 2012. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
                                                                  Richard L. Kaysen, Mayor 
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
 
 
 
 
              . 
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
BURNS SLIB City Consensus Resolution 11-2012.docx 

Town of Burns 
PO Box 66 
Burns, WY 82053 
307-547-2206 
 
 
October 9, 2012 
 
 
Office of State Lands and Investments 
Herschler Building, 3rd West 
122 West 25th St. 
Cheyenne, WY  82002 
 
 
To Whom It May Concern: 
 
The Town Council and I are very supportive of the request to allow transference 
of the unspent $23,299.86 remaining under grant number MRG-11232 to our 
new request which will allow the Town of Burns to replace an outdated but 
needed monitoring system which covers our water and sewer functions, 
including monitoring of sewer flow, lift station operation, flume output, well 
function, water storage tower levels, and chlorine analysis. 
 
This replacement has been strongly urged by the engineering firm with which 
the Town works, and was forwarded to the Council for action by our Utility 
Board.   
 
Thank you for your consideration. 
 
 
 
 
Judith Johnstone, Mayor 
Town of Burns 
 
 
 
 

 
OFFICE OF STATE LANDS AND INVESTMENTS 
 
Instructions to complete the Application Summary Form for Governmental Entity Grant 
and/or Loan assistance. 
 
An application Summary Form is to be completed and accompany each application when 
applying for funding from one or more of the following programs: 
 
Federal Mineral Royalty Capital Construction Account Grants (MRG) 
  
Wyoming Joint Powers Act Loans (JPA)
 
 
The Office of State Lands and Investments will reproduce and provide the completed 
Summary Form to the State Loan and Investment Board as the applicant’s summary 
of the request for funding.  The applicant should view this form as its opportunity to 
inform the Board of the need for the project and reason the applicant feels the state should 
help in financing the project.  There is no lim it on the length of the Application Summary 
Form, please attach additional forms if needed.  However, the Office suggests a bulleted 
format and the applicant should bear in mind t he time constraints of the State Loan and 
Investment Board. 
 
The narrative provided in the form should include at a minimum the following information: 
 
 
The type of entity the applicant is (city, county, water and sewer district, etc.). 
 
 
The approximate population of the applicant that the project will serve directly and 
indirectly. 
 
 
A brief description of the project and why the applicant needs the project. 
 
 
The requested amount from each funding source requested by the applicant and the 
percentage of the total project cost requested from each funding source. 
 
 
The applicant must also describe the source and the status of all matching funds.   
 
The applicant must provide an estimated project schedule that addresses planning, 
design and construction of the project. 
 
 
If the applicant submits multiple applications for consideration at one meeting, the 
applicant must provide its priority ranking of those applications. 
 
 
If the applicant needs the project to meet a federal or state health and/or a safety 
requirement the applicant must provide the specific health or safety requirements 
the project will address. 
 
 
If applying for a loan the applicant must list all sources of proposed security for the 
loan. 
 
For special districts, the following information is also required: 
 
 
The geographical area the special distri ct covers and the date when the County 
Commissioners formed the district. 
 
 
If the applicant is water and sewer district or an improvement and service district, 
the narrative must include the number and type of lots the project will serve initially 
and the total number of lots in the district, and the current zoning of the district.  For 
example, is the district zoned for residential use only or for commercial use or for a 
combination of uses? 

 
 
Office of State Lands and Investments - Government Grants and Loans 
 
Application Summary Form 
 
 
  
 
Date:     
 
 
 
 
 
ADDITIONAL INFORMATION PROVIDED BY THE APPLICANT 
 
 
APPLICANT:   
 
         
 
 
NAME OF PROJECT:   
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION: 
 
 
10/09/2012
Town of Burns
SCADA Upgrade and New Location
The Town of Burns is an Eastern Laramie County municipality of 301 residents. 
 
With our 2011-2012 lagoon project grant (MRG-11232) complete, we have not expended $23,299.86 of 
the funds that were allocated to that grant.   
 
Our initial request at the time of original filing was that we be allowed to roll any excess over to our 
Liberty Park project, but since that request we have been approved for Sixth Penny funding that will 
cover the entire Liberty Park development.   
 
Our new request for the excess funding is to allow us to spend the remaining dollars to upgrade and 
move our SCADA system from the Burns Community Center to the Burns-Plex which is now the home of 
our government offices.   The SCADA system monitors sewer lift stations, the flume at the lagoon, wells, 
and water storage towers 
 
Approval of this request will allow us to do an engineer recommended upgrade to our now obsolete 
SCADA system, which will include a new computer, antennas, chlorine analyzer, any other required 
associated equipment, and to install the upgraded system in the Burns-Plex.    
 
As soon as we receive approval for the project, we will begin the procurement process.  Design is not an 
issue because this will be a replacement of an obsolete computer and telemetry system with one with 
current technology.  The construction process will be mostly non-weather-dependent, since the 
computer will be installed in an office in our government complex, and the field monitoring equipment is 
mostly under cover as well, so the project will be completed as soon as equipment can be assembled, 
gathered, and installed. 
 
This will be the only funding for this project. 
 
 
 
 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2012-10-22_TotalExtraction_86869894.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. ________ 
ENTITLED:
"A RESOLUTION SUPPORTING THE CITY OF CHEYENNE’S
APPLICATION FOR A LAND AND WATER CONSERVATION
FUND GRANT FOR THE MARTIN LUTHER KING JR. PARK
QUICKSTART TENNIS COURT PROJECT."
WHEREAS, currently there are no tennis court facilities in Martin Luther King Jr. Park; and
WHEREAS, the area residents have strongly expressed their desire to see tennis court facilities in
this park; and
WHEREAS, if all this grant funding requests are awarded,  the Parks & Recreation Department, will
proceed with this project to build QuickStart  tennis courts on the west side of Martin Luther King
Jr. Park; and
WHEREAS, this project will include ADA accessible walkways to the tennis facility, as well as
ADA accessible gate entries and related amenities; and
WHEREAS, the estimated cost of the tennis project is $129,000; and
WHEREAS, a grant in the amount of $64,500 is being requested from the Land And Water
Conservation Fund; and 
WHEREAS, the Martin Luther King Jr. Park QuickStart Tennis Court Project is an eligible project
to receive a Land And Water Conservation Fund Grant and the required  match will come from “in-
kind”, the Cheyenne Parks & Recreation budget, earmarked Ward I contingency funds, a grant from
the United States Tennis Association, contributions from the Wyoming District United States Tennis
Association, and the Laramie County Recreation and Planning Advisory Board; all totaling $64,500.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF
CHEYENNE, WYOMING:
1. That the City of Cheyenne Governing Body supports a Land & Water Conservation Fund
(L&WCF) grant application for the Martin Luther King Jr. Park QuickStart Tennis Court Project and
that the match will be will be provide as described above.
2. That the Mayor of the City of Cheyenne, or other City official designated by the Mayor, is
authorized to sign an application for a L&WCF grant for a tennis court project in Martin Luther King
Jr. Park.  Further, that the Mayor is authorized to execute a grant agreement, in the event grant funds
are awarded to the City of Cheyenne for the Martin Luther King Jr. Park QuickStart Tennis Court
Project.    
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS_________ DAY OF November, 2012.
___________________________
Richard L. Kaysen, Mayor
(Seal)
ATTEST:
__________________________________
   Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk
X: L&W CF Grants: L&W CF 12 Res QSTennis.wpd

Martin Luther King Jr. Park QuickStart Tennis Court Project
The Land & Water Conservation Fund Grant guidelines require that a resolution of support from
the Governing Body be provided, along with our grant request to Wyoming Department of Parks &
Cultural Resources.   We are requesting $64,500. 
The L&WCF is Federal money that come through the State and is awarded once each year on a
competitive basis, with very specific criteria.  The projects to be considered must be “outdoor
recreation”.
The grant requires a 50/50 match.  The estimated cost of the project is $129,000.  We are
proposing that our local match of $64,500 be supplied through the Parks & Recreation Department
funds and “in-kind” in the amount of $30,000, City of Cheyenne Ward One Contingency Funds of
$8,197, a grant from the USTA of $23,240, and other grant funding of $3,063; totaling $64,500.
Our project will include four QuickStart tennis courts.  These tennis courts are smaller and are
perfect for children learning to play tennis.  This approach is a new play format for kids ages ten
and under, to introduce them to the sport of tennis. These courts are also popular with seniors,
those with bad knees, obese people, and the mobility-challenged.  Many people will benefit from
this new format of play.  Four QuickStart courts fit on the same size space as one full-size adult
tennis court.  The United States Tennis Association is strongly advocating this court style.  
A resolution of support for this grant request is required for consideration
L&WCF 12 Fin Com notes QS Tennis.wpd

RESOLUTION NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED:  "A 
RESOLUTION 
DETERMINING 
THAT 
CONDITIONS 
FOR 
ANNEXATION REQUIRED BY WYO. STAT. §15-1-402 EXIST FOR A 
PARCEL OF LAND BEING SITUATED IN A PORTION OF THE EAST 
HALF OF THE EAST HALF OF THE SECTION 20, TOWNSHIP 14 NORTH, 
RANGE 66 WEST OF THE 6TH P.M., LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING, 
(LOCATED APPROXIMATELY 150 FEET WEST OF THE INTERSECTION 
OF CONVERSE AVENUE AND OGDEN ROAD)." 
 
WHEREAS, a public hearing to receive public input and comments on the proposed 
annexation of the above described property was held before the governing body on October 22, 2012; 
and 
 
WHEREAS, the governing body finds that the following conditions exist as set forth in 
Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402: 
(i) 
The annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town; 
(ii) 
The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town; 
(iii) 
The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city 
or town and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to 
residents of the city or town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to 
be annexed; 
(iv) 
The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with the City of Cheyenne; 
(v) 
The City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electric utility, and its 
governing body is prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve 
the annexed area pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §15-1-410; 
(vi) 
The City of Cheyenne, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public 
hearing required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all 
landowners and affected public utilities within the territory a summary of the 
proposed annexation report as required under subsection (c) of Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 
and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing required by Wyo. Stat. 
§15-1-405(a). 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the above described property meets the requirements for 
annexation under Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 (a) (i-vi) and that conditions and required procedures have 
been met to proceed with annexation of a parcel of land being situated in a portion of the East Half of 
the East Half of the Section 20, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie 
County, Wyoming. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS _____ DAY OF _________, 2012. 
 
 
 
________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
RICHARD L. KAYSEN, MAYOR

ATTEST: 
 
 
 
__________________________________ 
CAROL INTLEKOFER, CITY CLERK

Zoning:  The property is zoned A-1. Proposed zoning has not been indicated as of yet.  
 
 

Annexation – A-12-0000 – Scenic Development 
  
Staff Report 
October 22, 2012 
 
Page 3 
 
SUMMARY: 
Staff recommends approval of this annexation resolution of compliance.  This resolution resolves 
that the minimum statutory criteria can be met with the proposed annexation.  The criteria are 
specifically addressed later in this report.  This proposal is for a multifamily project expected to 
be over 200 units.  This sort of land use is consistent with future land use plan and begins a 
logical transition from a larger commercial center to more neighborhood scale residential and 
commercial development.  The transportation network described in conjunction with this pro-
posal (see Attachment A) does propose deviations from the master transportation plan.  In 
general the transportation plan addresses primarily collector and arterial streets which are the 
larger streets in the community.  The plan helps to establish predicable expectations for both 
landowners and for the City in terms of anticipating where improvements to the transportation 
system will be necessary in order to efficiently and safely serve the community.  The transporta-
tion network will be specifically evaluated during the platting stage of the process.  A zoning 
process will also be brought before the City Council in conjunction with this proposal.   
 
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN DESIGNATIONS:   
LANDUSE: The land-use 
plan 
in 
PlanCheyenne 
indicates Mixed Use Com-
mercial Emphasis for this 
area and generally contem-
plates a transition from 
higher intensity commercial 
to more residential uses to 
the north.  In general the 
proposed development of 
multifamily developed to 
City standards is consistent 
with 
the 
comprehensive 
plan provided appropriate 
zoning is pursued.   
TRANSPORTATION:  
The master transportation 
plan establishes a network of collector and arterials and a corresponding traffic model is also 
developed to plan for a future transportation system that can be effectively maintained by the 
City. The transportation and landuse plans also establish reasonable expectations for urban 
landuses and future transportation improvements by surrounding landowners. 
The applicant has submitted a network plan for Section 20 that contemplates significant devia-
tions for the master transportation plan. (See Attachment A.) Significant deviations in this 
network plan include Rue Terre no longer connecting to Storey Blvd. and the east-west collector 
is no longer connecting at Converse with a four-way intersection. In addition, off-setting Cutoff 
Road from Cabin Trail road does not appear optimal for this undeveloped area. A network plan 
will be required with the preliminary plat and subject to a full review at that time.    
The transportation network in Section 20 has proven difficult with the incremental development 
patterns including many unique circumstances in the developed area including but not limited to: 
Figure 1 PlanCheyenne, Future Land Use Map 

Annexation – A-12-0000 – Scenic Development 
  
Staff Report 
October 22, 2012 
 
Page 4 
 
high traffic cut-through behind K-mart and through the Sam’s Club parking lot with routine 
counts of over 1,000 and 4,000 vehicle trips per day respectfully.  
This annexation alone does not preclude either the substantial compliance with the master trans-
portation plan or validation of the deviation. However, significant deviations from the 
transportation plan should require validation of a transportation study for both functionality and 
goals of the community otherwise reflected in the adopted plans.  Amending the master transpor-
tation plan and PlanCheyenne may also be required in conjunction with future development 
actions for this parcel. A PlanCheyenne Amendment would be appropriate in providing a process 
by which all members of the public affected by these proposed modifications may participate in 
the discussion. 

Annexation – A-12-0000 – Scenic Development 
  
Staff Report 
October 22, 2012 
 
Page 5 
 
DESCRIPTION OF SITE AND SURROUNDINGS:  The area being annexed is part of a 
larger area referred to as Section 20 (1 square mile or 640 acres) which is loosely bounded by 
Powderhouse, Storey, Dell Range and Converse. Development in this section has predominantly 
occurred incrementally, working its way north from Dell Range. Over 300 acres remain unde-
veloped in this section of land.  This annexation proposes to annex just under 20 acres for 
development as multifamily residential. 
Immediately to the south of this annexation are the back portions of Wal-Mart and Sam’s Club. 
To the east the annexation area is bounded by Converse Avenue and the back of two single 
family residential units on Grandview Court. The proposed annexation is about ¼ mile east of 
the existing Rue Terre and over ½ mile from Storey Blvd. 
The proposed street access onto Converse is precluded from a direct connection east into Buffalo 
Ridge, due to an existing stormwater detention pond on the east side of Converse. The applicant 
landowner is also developing additional stormwater facilities in the County to the north and east 
of this site near the existing Anderson School.

DEVELOPMENT PROCESS:  In general, future development on the proposed annexation 
area will need to go through both preliminary and final plat processes as well as an appropriate 
zone change. During the preliminary plat phase the transportation and drainage systems will be 
evaluated, including the previously mentioned transportation network plan. The proposed annex-
ation includes an approximately four-acre detention pond that will serve areas outside of the 
annexation and plat boundaries. This is unusual because the design of future drainage facilities 
will also dictate the future transportation and land use patterns. This complicates the matter 
further because the landowner in the un-annexed area may also pursue the development of 
stormwater facilities without City review in the County, similar to those developed near Ander-
son School. 
In addition to the platting and zoning process, the development of multifamily will also require a 
site plan, construction design, and associated design elements consistent with City Code. 
ANNEXATION ELIGIBILITY DATA/ANALYSIS:  Before any territory is eligible for an-
nexation, the governing body of any city or town, at a hearing as provided in W.S. 15-1-405, 
shall find that: 
1) The annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety, and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town. Findings:  The City of Cheyenne has 
adopted zoning and other City codes to assure new and existing development will provide 
for the health, safety and welfare of those future residents and users of the annexed area 
and the community as a whole.  By annexing, future landowners have agreed to comply 
with said regulations.  In addition the City has the ability to adopt, by ordinance, rules 
and regulations that provide assurances the City will continue to provide for the health, 
safety and welfare of the community into the future.  
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed constitutes a natural, geograph-
ical, economical, and social part of the annexing city or town. Findings:  The proposed 
annexation area is part of a larger area that is 100% contiguous to the City.  Adjacent 
roadways are under City jurisdiction and are maintained by the City.  Additionally the 
parcel is served by existing greenway also maintained by the City.  This area is included 
in PlanCheyenne for urban development.  The parcel is located adjacent to major com-
mercial and residential centers which include a variety of neighborhoods, schools, 
commercial centers and various services including medical.  City-run transit system is 
nearby as well.  
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or 
town and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to the residents 
of the city or town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. 
Findings:  This area is included in the City’s plans for extensions of City services, in-
cluding water and sewer, provision of City services and expansion of the transportation 
system.  To the degree that future development will be required to develop according to 
City regulations and consistent with adopted City planning documents, the annexed area 
is a feasible addition to the City 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with the annexing city or town, or the area 
meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. Findings:  The property is approximately 55% 
contiguous with existing City limits and part of a larger area that is 100% contiguous.  
The parcel shares approximately 1,000 feet with the existing City boundary and is located 
adjacent to an arterial roadway in the City.

5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is

6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public 
hearing required by W.S 15-1-405 (a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and af-
fected public utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as

REVIEWING AGENCY AND REVIEWERS COMMENTS: 
Board of Public Utilities:  
1. There currently are no water or sewer utilities to serve this property. The property Own-
ers shall be required to extend the sanitary sewer utilities from their current location to 
the farthest point (or points) of the property being annexed. These utility extensions shall 
be to a point (or points) that are logical for projected future connections to future devel-
opment projects, as determined by the BOPU and the property owners/developer. The 
sewer needs to be design to flow down to the sewer main behind the Wal- Mart Store and 
not to the Converse Ave. sewer main.  
2. A Wyoming Professional Engineer will need to submit proposed site plans with all utili-
ties and overall projected design, plans and profiles to the Board of Public Utilities. After 
BOPU’s review and signature of approval, the design engineer will need to send the plans 
to the (D.E.Q.) for approval and a permit to construct. When the D.E.Q. permit is issued, 
City’s Engineer’s Office and Cheyenne Fire Department have reviewed and signatures of 
approval are on the cover sheet. Then the Agent/Owner may employ a licensed contractor 
to install the utilities, apply for all applications for water/sewer tap permits from the

BOPU, and request a preconstruction meeting with all parties involved prior to construc-
tion. 
3. All BOPU’s water/sewer utilities shall be located in the City of Cheyenne Street Right-
of-Ways or have the appropriate easements record at the Clerk of Laramie County Real 
Estate Office or easements shown on the recorded plat.  
4. All water and sanitary sewer utilities need to be designed and constructed in accordance 
with the latest City of Cheyenne and Board of Public Utilities Construction Specifications

City Surveyor:  
1.  The ANNEXATION DESCRIPTION appears to correctly described the subject lands 
and closes mathematically. 
2.  In the CURVE TABLE the Chord Azimuth for C10 and C11 do not match the

4. NOT A REQUIREMENT: the ANNEXATION DESCRIPTION, CURVE TABLE and

in Bearing format.  My suggestion would be to have one format used for the plat.   
City Engineering:  
1. The soils report submitted does not meet the requirements of City Code Section 
1.16.030(B)(2). 
2. The annexation map submitted includes several errors: - Lots, blocks, and streets are 
shown on the annexation map that do not currently exist. - Converse Ave. to the east of 
the site is shown as being neither annexed nor non-annexed. The correct status is an-
nexed. 
3. This annexation map shows an 80' ROW of street proposed along the northern boundary 
of the site with a 302' centerline radius. This layout is consistent with a Collector. The 
developer should be aware that if the Transportation Network Plan required with the Pre-
liminary Plat submittal (UDC Section 4.3.2) shows future traffic levels that require the 
street to be a minor arterial, 100' of ROW will be required and the minimum centerline 
radius required will be 573'. 
Traffic Engineering:  
1. All information that is reasonably necessary to evaluate the application has not been 
submitted (ref. Article 3, Section 3.1.1.c, UDC). The application does not reflects sound 
planning and urban design principals with respect to existing and potential future devel-
opment in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future streets, open spaces, 
blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities (Article 2, Section 2.1.2.d.2, UDC). The 
long term improvement shall be those documented in the City’s Transportation Master 
Plan and any other Long term improvements adopted through the Cheyenne MPO and/or 
included in the State Transportation Improvement Program (Article 3, Section 3.1.3.f, 
UDC). This development requires a higher degree of specific planning based on the com-
plexity of the development, the difficulty of integrating development into the site or 

Annexation – A-12-0000 – Scenic Development 
  
Staff Report 
October 22, 2012 
 
Page 10 
 
vicinity, and the degree of flexibility desired from generally applicable design standards 
(Article 2, Section 2.2.2.s, UDC). 
2. The classification and alignment of the roadways on the Conceptual Road Network ap-
pear to have been determined without the benefit of the Master Transportation Planning 
Study. The conceptual Road network has never been formally submitted and is not con-
sistent with any previously approved regulating plan or development master plan. With 
no projected build-out of the area, it would appear that the growth in vehicle traffic will 
far outpace any transportation system roadway improvements necessary to mitigate ca-
pacity and access issues. It is requested that a Master Transportation Planning study be 
prepared. A Master Transportation Study for the remaining undeveloped area in the 
Square mile between Storey Boulevard and Dell Range Boulevard and Powderhouse 
Road and Converse Avenue should precede the Transportation Impact Study for the 
apartment project. 
3. It is imperative that the studies be completed and provided for review and approval to in-
sure that the right-of-way for future use is preserved and that the proposed infrastructure 
will provide capacity and access to accommodate all subsequent future growth. It is im-
portant that the alignment, right-of-way needs, and design details of streets and highways 
be identified well ahead of actual development so that proper planning of areas can occur. 
A Master Transportation Planning Study would identify the impacts associated with a 
phased approach development will have on existing and proposed transportation systems, 
both at the immediate location and in the general area and the milestones that trigger the 
need for additional improvements to mitigate those impacts. 
4. The Cheyenne Area Master Plan has a Buildout Roadway Vision Plan, herein referred to 
as the Plan. The Plan designates roadways and corridors that should be preserved for fu-
ture use. The proposed Conceptual Road network differs substantially from the Plan. For 
development to occur, the developer will need to build or fund construction of any road-
ways or infrastructure that is necessary to provide access, increase capacity and/or 
mitigate adverse impacts of development. Costs and responsibilities associated with 
transportation often lie entirely on developers in designing and building any infrastruc-
ture necessitated by the proposed development. 
5. When outside funding sources are necessary for implementation of improvements, a key 
consideration for inclusion in funding is whether the improvement primarily serves the 
development or whether it serves a larger area. Identification of needs ahead of develop-
ment helps to determine which improvements provide the best use of limited funds. The 
Master Transportation Planning Study requested and the Transportation Impact Study 
should include a list of improvements, costs and funding sources, including cost partici-
pation for funding consideration. The inclusion of this financial element aids in providing 
a balance between the transportation improvement projects recommended for implemen-
tation and the resources of the City, County, and MPO have available to build and 
maintain transportation facilities and services. 
6. The continued approval of Development Actions preceding an approved Master Trans-
portation Planning Study will only build on the success or failure of the existing roadway 
system. We need to take steps to prevent past mistakes from recurring. 
City/County Health: 
This property is located within the "201 Area" Facilities Management Plan area and is 
contiguous to the city limits.  The property shall utilize public sewer.

School District #1: 
This development site falls within the East Triad -- student’s home attendance boundaries 
are East High School, Carey Junior High School and Anderson Elementary Schools. 
The current attendance for these schools is as follows: 
Anderson Elementary - at capacity with no space available. 
Carey Junior High - below capacity with space available in both grades.

MPO: 
1. The Scenic Development annexation action site plan contains a road on the north side of 
the development named East Carlson. It appears that this road with a NW to SE align-
ment will be designated as a collector and built with an 80’ ROW. The MPO is concerned 
that this road in practicality will replace the E/W collector mapped in the adopted Master 
Transportation Plan (MTP) of PlanCheyenne, which would connect the 
Plainview/Converse intersection on the east to Powderhouse on the west. If the intention 
of the developer is to change the alignment of roads in the MTP then they need to go 
through the process of changing the Master Transportation Plan.  
2. As the City Governing Body has asked for in the past from this land owner, a master plan 
for the entire future developable area (Section 20) should be provided and a traffic study 
justifying the master plan change should be provided. There is another N/S collector from 
the MTP along the Rue Terre alignment that will be required to be shown on the sub area 
plan as well. All this needs to be take care of before or concurrently with the annexation 
of this property. 
Police: 
1. A 336 unit apartment complex may increase the population of the City by nearly 900-
1000 residents. The Cheyenne Police Department has averaged 1.7 police officers per 
1000 residents and the national average is 2.3 officers per 1000 residents. Thus, when this 
project is fully developed the PD should be allowed to budget and hire a minimum of 1.5 
officers to meet the demand of the additional calls for service. This addition should take 
place a year before expected project completion since it takes a year to recruit, hire and 
train a police officer.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2012-10-22_TotalExtraction_66669572.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. 
 
 
 
ENTITLED:  “A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE TO ADOPT THE REVISED STATEMENT OF INVESTMENT POLICY” 
 
 
WHEREAS, Wyoming State Statute 9-3-831(h) requires the adoption of a 
statement describing the City of Cheyenne’s (City’s) investment policy; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Resolution Number 3842, the Governing Body 
adopted a Statement of Investment Policy; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City’s Investment Committee has since revised the City’s 
Statement of Investment Policy to include the criteria set forth in Resolution 
Number 4021, which was adopted by the Governing Body on August 9, 1999, as 
amended in Resolution Number 4193, adopted on June 25, 2001, as amended in 
Resolution Number 4272, adopted on January 28, 2002; and as amended in 
Resolution Number 4974, adopted on August 27, 2007; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body wishes to adopt a Revised Statement of 
Investment Policy as proposed by the Investment Committee. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the revised Statement of Investment Policy, 
which is attached, is hereby adopted and made effective upon passage of this 
Resolution. 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this  
 
  day of November, 
2012. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Richard L. Kaysen, Mayor 
 
(SEAL) 
 
 
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Carol A. Intlekofer, City Clerk 
 
 

1 
 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
STATEMENT OF INVESTMENT POLICY 
Originally adopted: August 9, 1999 
Revised: May 25, 2001 
Revised: January 28, 2002 
Revised: August 27, 2007 
Revised: XXXXXXXX, 2012  
 
1.0 GENERAL 
This Statement of Investment Policy is adopted pursuant to the requirements of Wyoming 
State Statutes 9-4-831(h), as amended. It is the policy of the City of Cheyenne to invest 
public funds in a manner which will provide a reasonable rate of return while assuring the 
maximum security of principal, meeting the operational cash flow demands of the City of 
Cheyenne, and conforming to all federal, state and local laws and regulations governing 
the investment of public funds. This Policy will apply only to long-term asset investment 
of City assets. The City will continue to use the State of Wyoming Local Investment Pool 
(WyoStar) as a primary means of short-term investment management. The Committee 
may, at its discretion, use other qualified short-term investment management vehicles or 
programs. 
 
2.0 SCOPE 
This Investment Policy applies to all long-term financial assets of the City of Cheyenne. 
These funds are accounted for in the City's Government-Wide Financial Statements. 
 
3.0 PRUDENCE 
Investments should be made pursuant to the Prudent Man Rule, which provides a flexible 
legal investment standard that requires a fiduciary to make investments that a reasonable 
man of discretion and intelligence would choose to earn a reasonable level of income and 
to preserve the principal. Under this standard, a Reasonable Man is defined as one who 
exercises those qualities of attention, knowledge, intelligence and judgment which 
society requires of its members for the protection of their own interest and interests of 
others. 
 
4.0 OBJECTIVES 
The primary objectives, in priority order, of the City of Cheyenne investment actions 
shall be: 
 
4.1 Safety: Safety of principal is the foremost objective of the investment 
program. Investments of the City shall be undertaken in a manner that seeks to 
ensure the preservation of capital in the overall portfolio. To attain this objective, 
diversification is required in order that potential losses on individual securities do 
not exceed the income generated from the remainder of the portfolio. 
 
4.2 Liquidity: The City's investment portfolio will remain sufficiently liquid to 
enable the City to meet all cash flow requirements which are reasonably 
foreseeable. 

2 
 
 
4.3 Return on Investment: The City's investment portfolio shall be designed with 
the objective of attaining a reasonable rate of return throughout budgetary and 
economic cycles, taking into account the City's investment risk constraints and the 
cash flow characteristics of the portfolio. 
 
4.4 Community Investment: The City's investment portfolio will be managed with 
a commitment toward the benefit of the community. To this end, investing in 
local institutions, domestic instruments, or through local brokers will be the 
preferred investment strategy. This strategy will be tempered with the City's 
fiduciary responsibility and policy of attaining a market rate of return. 
 
5.0 MANAGEMENT RESPONSIBILITY 
Management responsibility for the investment program is hereby delegated to the City 
Treasurer, who shall establish written procedures for the operation of the investment 
program consistent with this Investment Policy. Procedures should include reference to 
safekeeping, Public Securities Association (PSA) repurchase agreements, wire transfer 
agreements, collateral/depository agreements and banking service contracts. Such 
procedures shall include explicit delegation of authority to persons responsible for 
investment transactions. 
 
No person may engage in an investment transaction except as provided under the terms of 
this Policy and the procedures established by the City Treasurer. The City Treasurer shall 
be responsible for all transactions undertaken and shall establish a system of controls to 
regulate investing activities. 
 
6.0 INVESTMENT COMMITTEE 
In order to facilitate direct communication to the Governing Body, an Investment 
Committee shall be established. The Committee shall be composed of: 
 
1. Two members of the City Council appointed by the President of the City 
Council, one of whom shall serve as the Chairman of the Committee, for 2-
year terms. Initially, one appointment will be for a 1-year term and the other 
for a 2-year term; thereafter, terms will be for 2 years. 
2. The Mayor or designee (designee shall be a voting member of the 
Committee). 
3. The City Treasurer 
4. The Assistant City Treasurer (BOPU Administration Manager) 
5. The City Attorney (or his designee) (non-voting) 
6. Two non-City employees with financial expertise (non-voting) will be 
appointed by the Chairman of the Committee, for 2-year terms. Initially, 
one appointment will be for a 1-year term and the other for a 2-year term; 
thereafter, terms will be for 2 years. 
7. Administrative Staff will be provided by the City Council Office. 
 
The Investment Committee will meet on a quarterly basis or as needed at a time 
designated by the Investment Committee Chair. The Investment Committee shall review 

3 
 
investments at least quarterly.  The purpose of the Investment Committee as it relates to 
the Investment Policy shall be: 
 
1.  The periodic review of and report on the City's investment activities; 
2.  The periodic review of the City's Investment Policy; 
3.  Review and comment on any amendments of the City's Investment 
Policy as proposed by the City Treasurer; and 
4. Consider and make recommendations on other financial functions of the City. 
 
7.0 ETHICS AND CONFLICTS OF INTEREST 
Officers and employees involved in the investment process shall refrain from personal 
business activity that could conflict with proper execution of the Investment Policy, or 
which could impair their ability to make impartial investment decisions.  Officers and 
employees shall not conduct any personal investment business with the same branch 
office of the firm through which the City conducts any investment activity. "Personal 
Investment Business" used here does not mean checking accounts, savings accounts, 
money market mutual funds, time deposits of five years or less, insurance products, or 
deferred compensation programs.  Members of the Investment Committee employed by 
the City of Cheyenne shall disclose to the Mayor any material financial interests in 
financial institutions that conduct business within the jurisdiction, and shall further 
disclose any large personal financial/investment positions that could be related to the 
performance of the City's portfolio. 
 
8.0 AUTHORIZED FINANCIAL DEALERS AND INSTITUTIONS 
The City Treasurer will maintain a list of financial institutions, security broker/dealers 
and investment advisors (defined collectively as “investment service providers”) 
authorized to provide investment-related services to the City. Each must be: (a) properly 
registered with their appropriate regulatory agency; (b) possess insurance, either through 
private or regulatory sources, satisfactory to the Investment Committee; (c) have a 
satisfactory record of past performance; and (d) are registered to do business in the State 
of Wyoming.   
 
No public deposit shall be made except in a qualified public depository as established by 
Wyoming State Statutes 9-4-817 through 9-4-821, as amended. 
 
All investment service providers,  must supply the City Treasurer with some or all of the 
following: 
 
1. Audited financial statements; 
2. Proof of Financial Industry Regulatory Authority certification; 
3. Trading resolution; 
4. Proof of state registration; 
5. Complete broker/dealer questionnaire; 
6. Certification of having read the City's Investment Policy; and 
7. Appropriate executed contracts. 
 
A current audited financial statement may be required for each investment service 
provider with which the City invests. 

4 
 
 
 
9.0 AUTHORIZED AND SUITABLE INVESTMENTS 
The City Treasurer is authorized to invest in those types of securities prescribed in 
Wyoming State Statutes 9-1-416 and 9-4-831 as amended, and any other state law 
authorizing such investments. 
 
10.0 COLLATERALIZATION 
In addition to the collateralization requirements found in Wyoming State Statutes 9-4-821 
as amended, collateralization will also be required on the following types of investments: 
 
1. Time deposits; and 
2. Repurchase and reverse agreements. 
 
In order to anticipate market changes, amounts of funds on deposits and provide an 
adequate level of security for all deposited funds, the collateralization level will be 105% 
or higher as required by law, of the higher of the par or market value of the deposits. 
 
The market value of the deposits will include accrued interest. 
 
The adequacy of the pledged collateral will be measured by the market or par value of the 
collateral, whichever is the lesser. Quarterly reports on the deposits and securities must 
be submitted to the City Treasurer and the Chair of the Investment Committee, and shall 
include the following: 
 
1. Any accounts in a bank at the end of the quarter must be listed by due date, 
showing the principal amount, accrued interest and the interest rate; and 
2. A listing of the securities pledged by the bank to cover the City’s deposits.  
The list must include the type of security, the maturity date, the interest rate, 
the par value, the fair market value and the cusip number. 
 
The City may collateralize time deposits and repurchase agreements using longer dated 
investments not to exceed ten years to maturity. 
 
Authorized instruments for collateralization are those found in Wyoming State Statutes 9-
4-821 as amended, with the following exceptions: 
 
1. Conventional first mortgages. 
 
11.0 SAFEKEEPING AND CUSTODY 
Pledged collateral which are instruments of the United States Government and its 
agencies, will be safe kept by the Federal Reserve Bank. Other listed collateral that 
cannot be held by the Federal Reserve will be held by a non-affiliated, independent third 
party with whom the City has a current custodial agreement. 
 
A clearly marked evidence of ownership (safekeeping receipt) must be supplied to the 
City and retained. The City must receive a safekeeping receipt on the same day as the 

5 
 
deposit is made.  The right of collateral substitution upon prior notification and 
acceptance by the City is granted. 
 
All security transactions, including collateral for repurchase agreement, entered into by 
the City shall be conducted on a delivery-versus-payment (DVP) basis. 
 
12.0 DIVERSIFICATION 
The City will diversify its instruments by security type and institution. With the exception 
of US Treasury securities, obligations or securities issued by or guaranteed by any federal 
government agency or instrumentality, certificates of deposits, and authorized pools, no 
more than 50% of the City's total investment portfolio will be invested in a single security 
type.   
 
13.0 MAXIMUM MATURITY 
To the extent possible, the City will attempt to match its investments with anticipated 
cash flow requirements.   
 
The weighted average life of the City of Cheyenne 1-5 Year Short-Intermediate portfolio 
will not exceed four years. 14.0 ANNUAL AUDIT; INTERNAL CONTROL 
The City Treasurer may establish a process of annual independent review of the 
investment function by an external auditor. This review will provide internal control by 
assuring compliance with policies and procedures. 
 
15.0 MARKET YIELD 
The City's investment strategy is active. The City will generally purchase instruments 
with the intent of holding the investments until maturity.  However, active management 
generally acknowledges that securities can be sold prior to maturity for a variety of 
reasons including, but limited to: improving portfolio diversification and/or yield, 
changing portfolio composition, etc. 
 
Given this strategy, the basis used by the City Treasurer to determine whether reasonable 
yields are being achieved is if the rate of return on all average cash and investment 
balances is within 100 basis points (bps) below the lower of: 
 
1. The average one year certificate of deposit rate of local depositories; or 
2. The average annual earning rate of WYOSTAR as defined by Wyoming 
State Statutes 9-1-416. 
 
16.0 PERFORMANCE BENCHMARK 
Investment performance will be measured using “Total Rate of Return” measurement.  
This combines both the cash income and change in portfolio market value for all portfolio 
holdings to establish account performance.  
 
The assets of the portfolio managed by external Managers shall be invested with the 
objective of producing the following respective rates of return: 
 

6 
 
1. The “Cash” portfolio shall be managed to exceed the existing benchmark for 
the WyoStar program.  For cash portions of the portfolio not in WyoStar, the 
benchmark will be the Citigroup 3-Month Treasury Bill Index.     
2. The City of Cheyenne 1-5 Year Short-Intermediate portfolio shall be managed 
to exceed the annualized total return of the Merrill Lynch 1-5 Year 
Government Index.   
 
17.0 REPORTING 
The City Treasurer is charged with the responsibility of including a market report on 
investment activity and returns in the City Treasurer’s Financial Report on a monthly 
basis. 
 
18.0 INVESTMENT POLICY ADOPTION 
The City's Investment Policy shall be adopted by Resolution of the City's Governing 
Body. The Policy shall be reviewed at least annually by the Investment Committee and 
any modifications made thereto must be approved by the Governing Body. 
 
STATEMENT OF INVESTMENT OFFICER OR ADVISOR 
Pursuant to Wyoming State Statutes 9-4-831(h) as amended, the following Statement

City of Cheyenne Owned Ice and Events Center 
1530 W. Lincolnway 
Cheyenne, WY  82001 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE PARKS AND RECREATION DEPARTMENT 
CITY OF CHEYENNE OWNED ICE AND EVENTS CENTER  
RENTAL AGREEMENT 
 
 
This Agreement (“Agreement”), dated October 1, 2012 is by and between the City of 
Cheyenne, a municipal corporation, and pertains to the City of Cheyenne Owned Ice and Events 
Center, operated by the City Parks and Recreation Department, with offices located at 1530 W. 
Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming (“the City”) and Cheyenne Capitals Youth Hockey Association, a 
non-profit organization, having its principal office at P.O. Box 1791, Cheyenne, WY, 82003 
(“Renter”). 
 
WHEREAS, the City owns, manages, and operates the City owned City of Cheyenne Owned Ice 
and Events Center Complex  (the “Center”), located in Cheyenne, Wyoming, and has the power and 
authority to rent the use thereof to  others for the purpose of holding and presenting certain events; and 
 
WHEREAS, Renter desires to rent the use of certain Premises as indicated on Appendix A to this 
Agreement within the Center from the City, and the City desires to license the use thereof to Renter, for 
the purpose of holding and presenting youth hockey practices and games (the "Event"), all subject to the  
terms and conditions set forth in this Agreement. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the covenants and agreements contained herein, and intending 
to be legally bound hereby, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1.         Premises.  The City hereby grants to Renter permission to use those portions of the 
Center set forth on Appendix A, attached hereto and incorporated herein (the 
“Premises”), upon the terms and conditions contained herein.  Only Renter’s members, 
prospective members, designated representatives and invitees are permitted by this 
agreement to use the Premises. 
 
2.         Term.  The period of Renter’s use of the Premises shall be October 29th 2012 through 
March 10th 2013 (the “Term”).  In addition to the Term, the City shall make the Center 
available to Renter, with charge, for (i) preparation of the Event and delivery, move-in 
and set-up of Renter’s freight and other properties beginning at the time set forth on 
Appendix A, and (ii) load-out immediately following conclusion of the Event, ending no 
later than the time set forth on Appendix A.  Unless otherwise expressly stated herein, or 
otherwise agreed to in writing, all deliveries and items required for presentation of the 
Event shall be the sole responsibility of Renter.        
 
3.  
Purpose.  Renter shall be permitted to use the Premises solely for the purpose set forth in 
Appendix A.  Renter is not permitted to use the Premises for other purposes.    
 
4. 
Fees.  
(1) 
In consideration for the City granting Renter permission to use the 

Page 2 of 11 
Premises, Renter shall pay the City a rent fee according to the following 
rate schedule: 
 
 
 
 
. 
 
 
 
 
Prime Time 
 
 
 
 
Monday – Friday    4:00pm – 10:00pm $200/hr  
Saturday - Sunday  7:00am – 10:00pm $200/hr  
 
Non Prime 
Monday- Sunday   10:00pm – 7:00am $135/hr  
Monday – Friday   7:00am – 4:00pm   $150/hr 
 
Renter will not be charged for ice resurfacing.  The City will only 
resurface ice at its discretion. If extra ice resurfacing is requested 
during practice, games or tournaments, the Renter will be charged the 
sum of $50.00.   
 
 
(2)       Renter shall be allowed to position four trophy cases in the building to 
display trophies and awards won by the association. Trophies displayed 
must be contained inside of the cabinets and not on top. Renter must clean 
and maintain all cases. The City assumes no liability for loss or damage to 
the cases or their contents.  
 
(3)       The City will allow Renter to display up to fifteen sponsor banners not to 
exceed 3 x 5ft at the northeast corner of the building. The Renter must 
place a banner over the area identifying the area to be Cheyenne Capitals 
sponsors and not advertisers. All sponsor banners will be pre approved 
through the City prior to installation.  
 
(4)       Renter shall be allowed to place an 8 x 12ft temporary structure on a pre 
designated area on the east side of the building for storage. The City 
assumes no liability for theft, damage or loss of either the structure or its 
contents. Renter agrees that in the event of damage or vandalism repairs 
shall be made within twenty four hours.  
 
(5) 
Renter shall be responsible for reimbursing the City in full, promptly for 
the cost of repairing any and all damage resulting from Renter's use of the 
Premises or Center, excepting normal wear and tear.  Those who are found 
to have caused physical damage to the Center facility will be responsible 
for paying for damages to replace damaged or destroyed property.   The 
City reserves the right to bar any person from use of the Center or Premise 
after consultation with the Renter. 
 
(6)  
In the event that Renter fails to make complete payment of any of the 
above license fees on or before the specified payment dates, the City may 
add finance charges equal to 1½% per month (18% per annum) on such 
unpaid amounts.  In the event of default of payment pursuant to Section 18 
below, The City reserves the right to terminate this Agreement and retain 
all fees previously paid by Renter.  Costs of collection and reasonable 

Page 3 of 11 
attorneys’ fees incurred in connection therewith shall be paid by Renter. 
 
 
5.  
The City’s Responsibilities.  The City agrees to provide the following 
during Renter’s periods of use of the Premises. 
 
(1)  
The Premises for use by Renter. 
 
(2)  
Electricity and other utilities for lighting, heating, and air conditioning, as 
customarily provided by the City for ice skating events at the Center. 
 
(3) 
Cleaning and janitorial services. 
 
(4)  
All necessary support services, including all ancillary staff necessary to 
hold the Event at the Center, as customarily provided by the City for ice 
skating events at the Center. 
 
 
 
6.  
Renter’s Responsibilities. Renter agrees to comply with the following terms and 
conditions during its use of the Premises.  
 
(1)  
No alcoholic or malt beverages or glass containers shall be brought on the 
Premises. 
 
(2) 
Renter shall not be permitted alcohol at any event. 
 
(3) 
Each hockey players, while on the ice, shall wear a helmet and necessary 
protective equipment. 
 
(5)  
Renter shall not, without the City’s prior written consent: (i) collect spectator 
admission charges; (ii) sell or give away merchandise, equipment, food, 
beverages or any other items; (iii) engage in any type of commercial or fund 
raising activity on or about the Center or Premises.  Renter shall submit to the 
City a written request for any of the above activities.  
 
(6) 
Renter shall not permit any member of its group to be present on the ice while the 
ice resurfacing machine, is in operation. 
 
(7) 
Renter shall strictly adhere to the dates and the starting and finishing times of its 
permitted periods of use as set forth in the ice schedule.  Any practices or games 
that run past scheduled times will be billed at the applicable ice rate.  
 
(8) 
Renter shall comply with all rules and regulations posted upon the Center. 
 
(9)  
Renter agrees not to do any act or allow any act to be done during the Term of this 
Agreement which shall mar, deface or injure any part of the Center or Premises. 
Upon expiration of the Term, Renter shall deliver to the City the rented Premises 
aforesaid in as good a condition and repair as the same were at the beginning of 
the Term, excepting usual normal wear and tear.  

Page 4 of 11 
 
(10)  
Renter shall use the Center and Premises only in a safe and careful manner.  
 
(11)  
Renter shall be assigned locker rooms by the City for practices.  Locker room 
keys must be checked out from the front desk with collateral.  Keys will not be 
checked in or out by minors. The City will check the locker room after use for 
damage.  After inspection, if the locker room is found to be reasonably free of 
trash and damage the Renter will receive collateral in return for locker room keys.  
Locker room care is the responsibility of the Renter. Any damages to locker 
rooms will be billed to Renter. Collateral will under no circumstances be returned 
without return of locker room keys. 
 
(12)  
Renter shall notify the City in writing of any cancellation of practices at least 
seventy-two (72) hours prior to the date of the practice.  The Renter will be 
charged for the scheduled ice time if notification of cancellation or any alteration 
to the schedule is not communicated in writing to the City at least seventy-two 
(72) hours in advance.   In addition, Renter must notify the City in writing not less 
than fourteen (14) days of the cancellation or alteration of the schedule of any 
scheduled hockey tournament.  If the Renter does not provide fourteen (14) days 
notice of a cancellation or alteration of a tournament schedule, the City reserves 
the right to charge for ice time in accordance with this Agreement.  Tournament 
schedules must be submitted to the City by no later than twenty-one (21) days 
prior to the start date of the tournament.  In the event of inclement weather (city 
closure, road closure) an exception may be granted provided that funds paid to the 
Cheyenne Capitals for tournament play is refunded to the participants. Decisions 
on inclement weather cancellations will be at the discretion of the City. 
 
(13)     Tournament schedules shall not be published until approved by the City.  The City 
shall provide approval or changes within two (2) business days to the Renter. 
 
(14)  
Renter shall be responsible for the collection of funds or registration fees for their 
programs.  The City will not accept payment on behalf of the Capital Youth 
Hockey Organization unless otherwise stated in Appendix B. 
 
(15)  
The birthday party room located in the lobby is the City’s property.  This room is 
available for Renter’s use with a charge of $25.00 per hour at the discretion of the 
City.  One monthly board meeting prescheduled on a Monday thru Thursday 
evening will be allowed to take place without charge. Each meeting shall last no 
longer than three (3) hours and shall begin no later than 7 p.m. The room must be 
left in a clean condition.  Prior to use, the Renter shall coordinate the scheduling 
of this room with the Center’s Recreation Coordinator - Ice and Events.  Any 
scheduled use outside of the above mentioned times or by persons other than 
Renter or its authorized representatives will be charged the hourly rate. The City 
will make the Party Room available for Renter use as a hospitality room without 
charge during tournaments held at Center until 10:00 am. Renter will clean the 
room. The Renter must remove all items from the building immediately.    
 
(16)  
During tournaments, the Renter will not have exclusive use of the entire facility 
when ice is rented for $200/hr.  Thus, there are certain areas such as the lobby, 

Page 5 of 11 
arcade, and birthday party rooms that will remain open for regular business such 
as birthday parties, laser tag, etc.  Renter may exclusively rent the entire facility at 
regular rental rates if exclusive use of the entire facility is desired.  For 
tournament use, the Renter will have exclusive use of inner arena areas including 
ice surface, spectator stands, and locker rooms. Arena areas of ingress and egress 
shall remain available for use by the City. 
 
(17)  
For use of The City’s copy machine, Renter will be charged $0.20 per black and 
white copy and $0.30 per color copy.  Renter will be invoiced on a monthly basis. 
 
(18)     Complaints by Renter’s clients must be first directed to the Renter and then the 
Renter can direct the complaints to the City through the Cheyenne Capitals 
President.  The Renter will educate its participants regarding proper channels of 
communication. 
 
(19)     Outside food and beverage will not be allowed into the facility without the express 
written permission of the Recreation Coordinator - Ice and Events. 
 
 
7.  
Concessions. The City specifically reserves to itself and its usual Center concessionaires 
the exclusive right to provide or sell, and retain the proceeds from the sale of all food and 
beverages and all other concessions at the Event. All concessions of food and beverage 
provided to Renter or its guests, invitees, or other Event attendees, shall be at an

8.  
Indemnification; Governmental Immunity. The Renter agrees to defend, hold harmless, 
and indemnify the City, and its officials, employees, authorized agents and volunteers 
against any and all claims and costs, including attorney’s fees, resulting from the Renter’s

a.

c. 
It is understood and agreed that these policies, except for worker's compensation 
or employer's liability, are primary and not contributory. All policies required under this contract shall

this Agreement.  It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Renter to immediately notify in writing the

d 
The City of Cheyenne shall be named as an additional insured on Renter's

e. 
The City has the right to reject a certificate of insurance if Renter's insurer is

e.. 
The City has the right to review the insurance certificates of any or all

The City has the right to increase the required minimum limit of liability on any

1. handling of hazardous materials

g. 
The City shall have the right to consult with Renter's insurance carriers and agents

1. exclusions

10.  
Security.  
 
(1)  
If requested by the City, Renter shall provide and pay for its own security 
personnel in sufficient numbers to police Renter’s use of the Premises and any 
spectators, guests or invitees attending Renter’s Event. Any security personnel 
to be provided by Renter must be licensed in accordance with Chapter 5.48 of 
the Cheyenne City Code and approved in writing by the City and the City’s 
Chief of Police, or authorized designee, no later than ten (10) days in advance of 
Renter’s use of the Premises.  In the event the City requests Renter to provide 
such security and Renter fails to obtain licenses required by law or the City’s 
approval of personnel in a timely fashion or fails to provide adequate security, 
as determined by the City in its sole discretion, the City shall have the right to 
provide security personnel at regular rate for weekday duty and at double-time 
rate for weekend duty to be paid by Renter, or, alternatively, shall have the right 
to terminate this Agreement.  
 
(2)  
If, in the sole judgment of the City, Renter’s use of the Premises at any time 
poses a danger to the safety of any other user of the Premises or City employees

the right to eject from the Premises any person making use of the Premises

pursuant to this Agreement.  
 
11.  
Responsibility for Safety. The City shall be responsible to inspect the Premises subject to

any potential problems or defects.  Moreover, Renter shall be responsible for all crowd

Assumption of Risk. Renter, and all persons using the Center and Premises hereunder, for

Renter is leasing the Premises for the purpose of playing hockey, Renter acknowledges 
that these risks and dangers include, but are not limited to, the danger of being injured by

13.  
Control of Premises. The City and its authorized representatives shall have the right at all

the City's representative as to the number of persons that can safely and freely move 
about in the Premises shall be final.

hereunder, and may, during the Term, use any or all portions of the Premises not 
specifically licensed to Renter hereunder.  The City reserves the right to eject from the 
Center and/or Premises any person for any reason which the City, in its sole discretion,

15.  
Licenses. Renter shall secure in advance, prior to commencement of the Term, all 
licenses and permits that may be required by or in connection with use of the Premises 
for the Event, including without limitation any licenses or permits required by State law, 
the Cheyenne City Code, and by any performing arts societies, such as ASCAP, BMI 
and/or SESAC.  All licenses and permits must be present on Premises during the entire 
Event.    
 
16.  
Promotional Activity. Renter agrees not to publicly conduct any promotional activity and 
not to advertise any Event to be conducted at the Center without the prior written consent 
of the City.  If promotion and/or advertising of the Event is permitted by the City, all 
advertising and promotion of the Event shall be the responsibility of Renter, at its sole 
cost. Renter shall be permitted to advertise and/or promote season registration with 
fourteen days written notice to the City. 
 
17.  
Termination. In the event Renter at any time fails to comply with any of the terms and 
conditions of this Agreement, and fails to cure such non-compliance within five (5) days 
of its receipt of written notice by the City describing such noncompliance, the City may 
terminate this Agreement and retain as damages any fees paid by Renter under this 
Agreement, without prejudice to any other legal rights or remedies the City may have.    
 
18.  
Assignment. Renter may not assign its right to use the Premises to any other person, 
group or organization without the express written consent of the City, which the City may 
grant or withhold, in its sole discretion.  
 
19.  
Independent Contractors. The relationship between the City and Renter is that of 
independent contractors and not agents or employees.  Under no circumstances shall this 
rent Agreement be considered a contract of partnership or joint venture.  The City shall 
have no liability for any of the debts, accounts, obligations or other liabilities of the 
Renter, and Renter shall have no authority to obligate or bind the City in any manner.  
 
20.  
Force Majeure. In the event the City is unable to perform any of the terms or conditions 
of this Agreement due to events or causes outside the City’s control, such as destruction 
or damage to the Center or Premises or unfitness of the Center or Premises for occupancy 
as a result of fire or  act of God, riot, labor, strike, national or local emergency, or other 
cause not entirely within the control of the City, the City shall not be deemed to be in 
breach of this Agreement or liable to Renter for damages, but shall return to Renter any 
unused portion of the rent fees paid to the City by Renter, less the cost of any expenses 
incurred by the City hereunder.  
 
21.  
Applicable Law. This Agreement shall be construed and enforced in accordance with the 
laws of the State of Wyoming and may be enforced in any court of competent jurisdiction 
in the State of Wyoming.

22.
