# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2012-10-22_TotalExtraction_19529259.pdf
CONTRACT BETWEEN THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND RBC GLOBAL ASSET 
MANAGEMENT (U.S.) INC. 
 
This contract is entered into by and between the City of Cheyenne, a municipality duly 
organized and existing under the laws of the State of Wyoming, whose address is 2101 O’Neil 
Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming  82001, hereafter (“City”), and RBC Global Asset Management 
(U.S.) Inc., whose address is 100 South Fifth Street, Suite 2300, Minneapolis, Minnesota 55402-
1240, hereafter (“Advisor”). 
 
1. 
Purpose of Contract.  The purpose of this contract is to authorize the City to maintain an 
investment account (the “Account”) with the Advisor and authorizes the Advisor to manage all 
assets from time to time held in the Account (the “Assets”).  The City hereby appoints the 
Advisor as its investment advisor with respect to the Assets, and delegates to the Advisor the 
authority to manage, acquire and dispose of the Assets.   
 
2. 
Term.  This Contract is effective when all parties have executed it and all approvals have 
been granted.  The term of this Contract shall be through June 30, 2015.  By mutual agreement, 
this Contract may be extended for two (2) additional one (1) year periods. 
 
3. 
Payment.   The City shall pay to the Advisor, as full compensation for services rendered 
under this Agreement, a fee based on the value of the Assets, computed in accordance with 
Section 4.  This annual fee is set forth in Appendix B and shall be paid on a quarterly basis in 
advance.  For the purpose of calculating such fee with respect to any calendar quarter, the value 
of the Assets shall be determined as of each Appraisal Date for the completed prior calendar 
quarter.  If the Account is terminated during a calendar quarter, the fee will be prorated based on 
the period of time during the quarter that the Account was open and any unused portion of any 
fees paid in advance shall be returned to the City.  The Advisor shall not be responsible for, and 
shall be reimbursed by the City for, any and all custodial fees, and for all other reasonable out-
of-pocket costs and expenses incurred by the Advisor related to its performance under this 
Agreement.  The fees and expenses due to the Advisor under this Section 3 shall be payable 
within fifteen (15) days after the City’s receipt of the appraisal referred to in Section 4 and the 
Advisor's statement of the amount of fees and expenses due, but if not so paid shall be a lien 
upon and payable from the Assets. 
 
4. 
Appraisal of Account.  The Advisor shall provide the City with an appraisal of the 
Account, summarizing the Assets, including cash and accrued income for individual securities or 
net asset value for any funds, as of the last day of each calendar quarter on which the bond 
markets are open for trading (the “Appraisal Date”).  To the extent possible, each security shall 
be valued on the Appraisal Date at the last traded price for such security.  Other sources used for 
fixed income securities pricing shall include vendor-based evaluations, dollar price quotes from 

broker-dealers for identical securities, quoted prices for similar assets, prices calculated using 
observable inputs, index providers, proprietary pricing software, and matrix pricing.  Trades 
which have not been settled as of the Appraisal Date and any other Assets shall be valued at fair 
value as determined in good faith by the Advisor.  In making valuation determinations, the City 
understands and acknowledges that the Advisor may rely on the services of a third-party pricing 
service.  Notwithstanding anything to the contrary herein, valuation determinations shall be made 
using the Advisor’s own valuation methodology, unless otherwise agreed to in writing. 
 
5. 
City’s Responsibilities.  The City shall: 
 
i. 
Advise the Advisor in writing of the investment objectives of the Account and any 
investment restrictions applicable to the Account, and of any changes in such 
objectives or restrictions.  The initial written statement of investment policy, which 
shall be attached hereto as Appendix A, shall have the effect of limiting the 
investments the Advisor is authorized to make for the Account and shall be binding 
upon the Advisor.  This written statement of investment policy may be modified by 
the City at any time. The City shall communicate any such changes to the Advisor in 
writing at least ten business days before the date on which they shall become 
effective. 
 
ii. 
The City may at any time add Assets to or remove them from the Account; provided 
that certain lead time may be necessary between the time of the City’s notice to the 
Advisor of the City’s intent to transfer Assets from the Account and the subsequent 
removal of such Assets to allow for any necessary valuation by the Advisor of Assets 
to be transferred from the Account; and provided further that in no event shall such 
necessary lead time exceed a reasonable valuation period from the time the Advisor 
receives notice of the City’s intent to transfer assets from the Account. 
 
6. 
Authority of Advisor.   The Advisor shall have full and complete discretion to direct and 
manage the investment and reinvestment of the Assets, including the purchase, sale, exchange, 
conversion or other transactions in any and all securities (including, without limitation, bonds, 
debentures, notes, certificates of deposit, time deposits, banker's acceptances, repurchase 
agreements and mutual funds) with one or more broker/dealers which the Advisor shall select.  
Notwithstanding the foregoing, the Advisor shall not engage in short sales or margin 
transactions, without the prior written approval of the City. 
 
 
 
 
 

7. 
Representations and Warranties of the City.   
 
i. The City represents and warrants to the Advisor that the City has the requisite legal 
capacity and authority to execute, deliver and perform its obligations under this 
Agreement. 
 
ii. The City represents and warrants to the Advisor that this Agreement has been duly 
authorized, executed and delivered by the City and is the legal, valid and binding 
agreement of the City, enforceable against the City in accordance with its terms.   
 
iii. The City’s execution of this Agreement and the performance of its obligations under 
this Agreement do not conflict with or violate any provisions of any governing 
documents of the City, any applicable state or federal law, or any obligations by 
which the City is bound, whether by contract, operation of law or otherwise. 
 
8. 
Special Provisions. 
 
i. 
Custody of Securities/Transaction Procedures.  The Advisor shall not act as custodian 
for the Account and shall not take possession of any Assets.  The City shall maintain 
or establish, in the City’s name, an account with a broker/dealer, bank or trust 
company in which the City shall maintain or deposit the Assets.  The broker/dealer, 
bank or trust company selected by the City shall be the custodian of the Assets.  The 
City will cause the custodian to take all necessary steps to settle purchases, sales and 
trades made by the Advisor, including delivery of certificated securities, payments of 
funds and such other acts as may be necessary to fulfill such custodial 
responsibilities.  The Advisor shall give notice and directions with respect to 
transactions in a manner that shall be agreed upon with the custodian.  The Advisor 
shall not be responsible for any loss caused by any act or omission of the custodian.  
The City shall execute any and all documents that the Advisor may from time to time 
transmit to the City for the purpose of confirming securities transactions of the 
Account. 
 
ii. 
Allocation of Brokerage.  The Advisor may allocate portfolio transactions for the 
Account to one or more broker/dealers, which the Advisor shall select, for execution 
on such markets, at such prices and at such commission rates as in the Advisor’s good 
faith judgment will be in the Account’s best interest.  In so doing, the Advisor will 
consider not only available prices and commission rates, but also other relevant 
factors such as, without limitation, execution capabilities, research and other services 
provided by such broker/dealers which are expected to enhance the Advisor’s general 
portfolio management capabilities, the value of an ongoing relationship of the 

Advisor with such broker/dealers, and the full range and quality of such 
broker/dealers’ services, without having to demonstrate that any of the foregoing 
factors are of direct benefit to the Account.  The Advisor shall not be responsible for 
any loss caused by any act or omission of any broker/dealer; provided, however, that 
with respect to those broker/dealers that have been selected by the Advisor, the 
Advisor has acted prudently in the selection of such broker/dealers. 
 
iii. 
Reports.  The Advisor will send the City monthly reports regarding the Assets, 
including a summary of transactions executed and the valuation of the Assets.  The 
City shall provide to the Advisor or cause to be provided to the Advisor such 
information and reports relating to the Account and the services to be provided by the 
Advisor under this Agreement as the Advisor may reasonably request.  The Advisor 
is not responsible for the accuracy of such information and reports.  The records 
provided by the City, the City’s custodian or other third parties shall be the official 
records of the Account.  The City is urged to compare statements provided by the 
custodian of the Account with reports provided by the Advisor. 
 
iv. 
Confidentiality.  All information and advice furnished by the Advisor to the City 
under this Agreement shall be confidential and shall not be disclosed to third parties, 
except as required by law.  All information furnished by the City to the Advisor under 
this Agreement shall be confidential and shall not be disclosed to any unaffiliated 
third party, except as permitted or required by law, where it is necessary to effect 
transactions or provide other services to the City, or where the City requests or 
authorizes the Advisor to do so.  Further, in receiving holdings data, the City agrees 
that the data is not being obtained in order to affect securities transactions based upon 
such information or to provide such information to another party. 
 
v. 
Representation by the Advisor/Standard of Care.  The Advisor hereby represents and 
warrants that it is registered as an investment adviser under the Investment Advisers 
Act of 1940 (the “Advisers Act”), and shall take all actions necessary to remain duly 
registered under the Advisers Act.  The Advisor undertakes to perform only those 
duties that are specifically set forth in this Agreement.  Except for bad faith, 
intentional misconduct, negligence, or violation of any federal, state, or local law or 
professional standard of care in regard to the Advisor’s performance of its duties 
under this Agreement, neither the Advisor nor any of its directors, officers, 
employees or agents shall be liable for any acts or omissions or for any loss suffered 
by the Account.  The Advisor and its directors, officers, employees and agents shall 
be entitled to rely, and shall be protected from liability in relying, upon any 
information or instructions furnished to it (or any of them as individuals) which is 
believed in good faith to be accurate and reliable.  The City understands and 

acknowledges that the Advisor does not warrant any rate of return, market value or 
performance of any assets in the Account.  The federal securities laws impose 
liabilities under certain circumstances on persons who act in good faith and, therefore, 
nothing herein shall constitute a waiver of any right which the City may have under 
those laws. 
 
vi. 
Receipt by the City of  Part 2 of Form ADV.  The City acknowledges receipt of the 
Advisor’s Form ADV, Part 2. 
 
vii. 
Services for Other Cities.  The City acknowledges that the Advisor provides 
investment advisory services for other clients.  The City agrees that the Advisor may 
give advice or take action with respect to any of its other clients which may differ 
from advice given, or the timing or nature of action taken, with respect to the 
Account, so long as it is the Advisor’s policy, to the extent practical, to allocate 
investment opportunities to the Account over a period of time on a fair and equitable 
basis relative to other clients.  The Advisor shall have no obligation to purchase or 
sell for the Account any security which the Advisor, its directors, officers, employees 
or agents may purchase or sell for their own accounts or for the account of any other 
client, if in the Advisor’s opinion such transaction appears unsuitable, impractical or 
undesirable for the Account. 
 
9. 
Insurance.  The Advisor shall provide the City with proof of the following insurance 
coverages: 
 
 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
 
i. 
The Advisor shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $1,000,000 to protect the City 
from any and all claims arising from the Advisor’s negligence in the performance of 
duties under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be 
provided pursuant to an “occurrence” policy.  
 
ii. 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a "claims made" policy: 
 
a. the Advisor shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, provide the 
City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be 
in effect at the time the Advisor begins the provision of services under this 
Agreement; and 
 

b. In the event the Advisor's services extend into a future policy period, the Advisor 
shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City with a new certificate of 
insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be in effect during all 
periods of time that the Advisor will provide services under this Agreement; and 
 
c. the Advisor shall maintain said "claims made" coverage for a period of five (5) 
years following the last date that the Advisor has provided services under this 
Agreement; and  
 
d. In the event the Advisor or the insurer terminates "claims made" coverage prior to 
the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs a, b, or c of this 
paragraph, the Advisor shall provide to the City advance written notification of the 
termination of said coverage and shall provide the City with an endorsement for 
an extended reporting period ("tail coverage") which shall be in effect for a period 
of time not less than five (5) years following the last date that the Advisor has 
provided services under this Agreement. 
 
iii. 
Advisor shall provide the City with a certificate of insurance listing the City as 
certificate holder prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that this policy is primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Contract shall be in effect for the duration of the Contract.  
It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Advisor to immediately notify in writing the 
city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or 
occurrence that has resulted in or may result in the cancellation or substantive change 
of any insurance coverage required by this Contract, and failure to do so shall be 
construed to be a breach of this Agreement. 
 
The City’s failure to review the certificate of insurance shall not affect the City’s 
rights or Advisor’s obligation hereunder. 
 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Contract shall have a 
minimum A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
10. 
General Provisions. 
 
i. 
Amendments.  Any changes, modifications, revisions or amendments to this Contract 
which are mutually agreed upon by the parties to this Contract shall be incorporated 
by written instrument, executed and signed by all parties to this Contract. 
 

ii. 
Applicable Law.  The construction, interpretation and enforcement of this Contract 
shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of 
Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over this Contract and the parties, and the venue 
shall be in the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
iii. 
Compliance with Laws.  The Advisor shall comply with all applicable federal, state 
and local laws and regulations in the performance of this Contract. 
 
iv. 
Entirety of Contract.  This Contract and Attachments A and B represent the entire and 
integrated Contract between the parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, 
representations and agreements, whether written or oral. 
 
v. 
Indemnification.  The Advisor agrees to release, indemnify, defend and hold harmless 
the City and its officers, agents, employees, successors and assigns from any and all 
causes of action, claims, demands, liabilities, penalties, forfeitures and suits, and the 
costs and expenses incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, in any 
manner arising out of the Advisor's performance under this Contract, except to the 
extent such causes of action, claims, demands, liabilities, penalties, forfeitures and 
suits are directly attributable to the gross negligence or willful misconduct of the City. 
 
vi. 
Independent Contractor.  The Advisor shall function as an independent contractor for 
the purposes of this Contract.  The Advisor shall assume sole responsibility for any 
debts or liabilities that may be incurred by the Advisor in fulfilling the terms of this 
Contract. Nothing in this Contract shall be interpreted as authorizing the Advisor or 
its agents or employees to act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City 
or to incur any obligation of any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
vii. 
Notice.  Any notice, correspondence or billing required to be given by the terms of this 
Agreement shall be delivered by hand, or delivered by mail, postage prepaid, to the 
addresses of the respective parties stated above. 
 
viii. 
Nondiscrimination.  The Advisor shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the 
Wyoming Fair Employment practices Act, Wyo. Stat. §27-9-105 et seq., and the 
Americans with Disabilities Act (ADA), 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et. seq.  The Advisor 
shall assure that no person is discriminated against based on the grounds of age, race, 
sex, creed, color, national origin, ancestry, qualified disability, or pregnancy in 
connection with the performance of this Agreement. 
 

ix. 
Prior Approval.  This Contract shall not be binding upon either party, no services 
shall be performed under the terms of this Contract nor shall payment be made until 
this Contract has been approved by all necessary authorities. 
 
x. 
Governmental Immunity.  The City of Cheyenne specifically retains all immunities 
and defenses available to it as a governmental entity pursuant to the Wyoming 
Governmental Claims Act, Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101 et seq., and all other applicable 
laws.  Designation of venue, choice of law, enforcement action, and similar 
provisions should not be construed as a waiver of governmental immunity. 
 
xi. 
Termination.  Either party may terminate this Contract without cause upon thirty (30) 
days written notice to the other party.  Either party may terminate this Contract 
immediately with cause. 
 
xii. 
Default.  Each and every term and condition herein shall be deemed a material 
element of the Agreement.  In the event either party shall fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
xiii. 
Remedies.   
 
a.  In the event a party declares the other party in default hereof, said party declaring 
default shall notify the defaulting party in writing and such defaulting party shall be 
allowed a period of fifteen (15) days to cure said default.  In the event that the default 
remains uncorrected, the party not in default may elect to:  (a) terminate the 
Agreement and seek damages; (b) treat the Agreement as continuing and require 
specific performance; or (c) avail itself of any other remedy at law or equity. 
 
b. In the event the Advisor fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, 
the City may elect to make good such deficiencies and charge the Advisor therefore. 
 
xiv. 
Waiver.  The waiver by either party of any term, condition or covenant, or breach of 
any term, condition or covenant, shall not constitute a waiver of any other term, 
condition or covenant, or breach thereof. 
 
xv. 
Severability:  If any provision, section, subsection, sentence, clause, or phrase of this 
Agreement is invalidated by any court of competent jurisdiction, such holding shall 
not affect the validity of the remainder of this Agreement, which shall continue in full 
force and affect. 
 

xvi. 
Successors and Assigns.  All the terms, conditions, and provisions herein shall inure 
to the benefit of and be binding upon the parties hereto and their respective successors 
and assigns. 
 
xvii. 
Assignment.  Neither party shall assign this Agreement without prior written consent 
of both parties.  Any delegation or assignment shall not operate to relieve either party 
of its responsibilities hereunder.   
 
xviii. 
Third Party Rights.  The Parties do not intend to create in any other individual or 
entity the status of third party beneficiary, and this Agreement shall not be construed 
so as to create such status.  The rights, duties and obligations contained in this 
Agreement shall operate only between the parties to this Agreement, and shall inure 
solely to the benefit of the parties to this Agreement.  The parties to this Agreement 
intend and expressly agree that only the parties signatory to this Agreement shall have 
any legal or equitable right to seek to enforce this Agreement, to seek any remedy 
arising out of a party’s performance or failure to perform any term or condition of this 
Agreement, or to bring action for breach of this Agreement.  This paragraph is not 
intended nor shall it be construed to waive any of the parties’ immunities.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
[THE REST OF THIS PAGE IS INTENTIONALLY LEFT BLANK.] 
 
 
 
 
 

11. 
Signatures.  In witness thereof, the parties to this Contract, through their duly authorized 
representatives, have executed this Contract on the days and dates set out below and certify that 
they have read, understood and agreed to the terms and conditions of this Contract.  
  
This Contract may be executed in multiple counterparts, each of which shall be an 
original and all of which, when taken together, shall constitute one and the same Contract. 
 
The Contract date is the date of the last signature affixed to this page.

CITY OF CHEYENNE 
CONTRACT MODIFICATION 
 
Contract Modification: 
#1 
Project: 
 
 
Consultant Services – Environmental Monitoring 
Project Number: 
 
Contract #5511 
Contractor: 
 
 
TriHydro Corporation 
Date Prepared: 
 
8-29-2012 
 
The above reference contract is hereby modified in the manner set forth in the following and 
such amendments and modifications are agreed to by the undersigned, being parties to this 
contract and agreement.  All other aspects of the agreement remain unchanged and in full force 
and effect. 
 
I.  Description of Changes: 
 
Original Contract Amount: 
$209,220 
Modification #1: 
 
  209,220 
New Contract Total:  
$418,440 
 
II.  Contract Items:   
 
All items to remain the same as original agreement. 
 
III.  Schedule:  
 
Groundwater Sampling & Analysis:  Sampling shall be performed in the field on the following 
months: 
1)  April 2013 
2) August 2013 
3) December 2013 
4) April 2014 
5) August 2014 
6) December 2014 
7) April 2015 
8) August 2015 
9) December 2015 
 
Groundwater Gauging:  Groundwater gauging shall be performed in the field every sampling 
event, beginning in April 2013 and continuing through and including December 2015. 
 
Methane Monitoring:  Methane monitoring shall be performed in the field during the following 
months: 
1) March 2013 
2) June 2013 
3) September 2013 
4) December 2013 
5) March 2014 
6) June 2014 
7) September 2014 
8) December 2014 
9) March 2015 
10) June 2015 

11) September 2015 
12) December 2015 
 
Annual Reports:  Consultant shall submit a draft copy (electronic is acceptable) of a 
comprehensive annual report to the City by March 1 of each contract year.  Consultant shall 
incorporate City comments and submit one electronic and one paper copy of final report (if paper 
draft, page changes are acceptable) to the City and paper and electronic deliverables to WDEQ 
per their requirements by April 1 of each contract year. 
 
Event Reports:  Consultant shall submit a draft copy (electronic is acceptable) of a brief 
summary event report within 30-days of receipt of complete and accurate analytical results for 
each April, August and December monitoring event.  Consultant shall incorporate City 
comments and submit one paper copy and electronic deliverables to WDEQ per their 
requirements not later than 30-days following submission of draft.  
 
Compensation rates will remain the same as original contract as outlined in Exhibit B, 
Compensation.   
 
IV.  Summary:  
 
This modification increases the amount of the contract by $209,220 and extends the contract for 
one additional three-year period.  This modification includes groundwater sampling & analysis, 
groundwater gauging, methane monitoring and reports at the same frequency and number as 
included in original contract.  The total cost of this contract will not exceed $418,440 without a 
written change order signed by both parties.

City of Cheyenne

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2012-10-22_TotalExtraction_51402273.pdf
Second	  Amendment	  to	  MOU	  between	  Saddle	  Ridge	  and	  City	  of	  Cheyenne	  
1	  
SECOND AMENDMENT TO MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING 
BETWEEN THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND SADDLE RIDGE, LLC 
 
 
 
1. 
Parties.  This Second Amendment to Memorandum of Understanding (“Second 
Amendment”) is made and entered into by and between Saddle Ridge, LLC (“Saddle Ridge”), 
whose address is 506 Shoshoni Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82009, and the City of Cheyenne, a 
municipality organized and existing under the laws of the State of Wyoming (“City”), whose 
address is 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001. 
 
2. 
Recitals.  The purpose of this Amendment is to modify certain obligations under the 
Memorandum of Understanding (“MOU”), executed by and between Saddle Ridge and the City 
dated July 10, 2006 and as amended August 3, 2011, so that certain additions to the Saddle Ridge 
greenway system may be extended in a logical and chronological progression.  Unless otherwise 
indicated, references to Blocks and Lots in this Second Amendment are intended to refer to 
Blocks and Lots in Saddle Ridge Subdivision, Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
WHEREAS, Saddle Ridge has requested that the MOU be amended to permit Saddle 
Ridge to construct a portion of the Saddle Ridge greenway system, at its expense, in exchange 
for the assumption by the City of certain other greenway and sidewalk development duties and 
obligations. 
 
WHEREAS, the portion of the Saddle Ridge greenway system to be constructed by 
Saddle Ridge, pursuant to this amendment, is specifically described and identified as the 
pathway for the Saddle Ridge Greenway Development Area (“SRGDA”) running through and 
across Block 25, Lot 15, which portion is shaded in red on Exhibit “I” attached to this Second 
Amendment and incorporated herein. 
 
WHEREAS, under the MOU, Saddle Ridge is currently obligated to build a ten foot (10') 
greenway path within the Whitney Road Greenway Development Area (“WRGDA”), which 
pathway would be constructed within a utility easement along the eastern ROW of Whitney 
Road running from the south ROW of U.S. Highway 30 to the northern boundary of Lot 1, Block 
1, Saddle Ridge Fourth Filing, and running south from the connection of the pathways in the 
WRGDA and SRGDA to the north ROW of Pershing Boulevard.  These portions of the Saddle 
Ridge greenway system are highlighted in orange on Exhibit “I” attached hereto.  
 
 
NOW THEREFORE, the Parties agree to amend the MOU so that the City shall be 
responsible for design and construction of the WRGDA pathway from the south ROW of U.S. 
Highway 30 running south to the northern border of Lot 1, Block 1, Saddle Ridge Fourth Filing, 
as shown in orange highlighting on Exhibit “I”.  City shall also be responsible for the design and 
construction of the sidewalk running south from the connection of the SRGDA and WRGDA to 
the north ROW of Pershing Boulevard, as shown in orange highlighting on Exhibit “I”.  It is 
further agreed that Saddle Ridge shall now be responsible for construction of a portion of the 
SRGDA pathway running through and across Block 25, Lot 15, as shown in red highlighting on 
Exhibit “I”. 

Second	  Amendment	  to	  MOU	  between	  Saddle	  Ridge	  and	  City	  of	  Cheyenne	  
2	  
 
 
 
3. 
Amendments. 
 
 
 
Responsibilities of Saddle Ridge. 
A. 
Paragraph 6A(iv.) of the MOU shall be rewritten so that Paragraph 6A(iv.) shall 
read: 
“Install and complete the 10’ greenway path from the northern border of 
Lot 1, Block 1, Saddle Ridge Fourth Filing, to connect with the SRGDA pathway.  
Provide rough grading for WRGDA through and across Block 14, Lot 20.” 
 
B. 
Paragraph 6A(vi.) of the MOU shall be rewritten so that Paragraph 6 
A(vi.) shall read: 
“Pay for the construction cost of the 10’ greenway path from the northern 
border of Lot 1, Block 1, Saddle Ridge Fourth Filing, running south to connect 
with the SRGDA pathway.” 
 
C. 
Paragraph 6B shall be rewritten to include subsection “iv.” so that the MOU shall 
now include and incorporate the following language: 
“iv.  Shall construct a 10’ wide greenway path through and across Block 25, Lot 15 (as 
identified in Exhibit “I”) with the exception of the already completed pathway. 
 
Responsibilities of City. 
 
D. 
Paragraph 7A shall be rewritten to include subsection “v.” so that the MOU shall 
now include and incorporate the following language: 
“v.  Install and complete the 10’ greenway path from the south ROW of U.S. Highway 30 
running south to the northern border of Lot 1, Block 1, Saddle Ridge Fourth Filing. 
Design of the path through and across Block 1, Lot 1 shall be negotiated between the 
developer and commercial buyer and the Parks and Recreation Department to make 
reasonable accommodations for the steep topography, which may include construction of 
the greenway path outside of the existing easement not to exceed what is reasonably 
necessary.  The City shall make its best efforts to design and build the greenway path 
within the existing easement.”  
 
 
E. 
Paragraph 7A shall be rewritten to include subsection “vi.” so that the MOU shall 
now include and incorporate the following language: 
“vi.  Pay for the construction cost of the 10’ greenway path from the south ROW of U.S. 
Highway 30 running south to the northern border of Lot 1, Block 1, Saddle Ridge Fourth 
Filing.  
 
vii.  The City shall, at its expense, design and construct a sidewalk running from the 
connection of the pathways of the WRGDA and SRGDA to the northern ROW of 
Pershing Boulevard.”  
 

Second	  Amendment	  to	  MOU	  between	  Saddle	  Ridge	  and	  City	  of	  Cheyenne	  
3	  
 
F. 
Paragraph 7C(i.) shall be rewritten to limit the scope of work of construction of 
the 10’ wide trail only to Block 20, Lot 13 and what has already been constructed through Block 
25, Lot 15, so that Paragraph 7C(i.) shall read: 
 
“Provide all of the remaining improvements to the SRGDA including plantings and 
irrigation.  City is responsible for the construction of the 10’ wide trail through Block 20, Lot 13, 
and already constructed portion through Block 25, Lot 15, to the Fireside Drive cutoff.” 
 
 
G. 
Exhibit “I” to this Second Amendment, attached hereto and incorporated herein, 
shall reflect the foregoing textual amendments to the MOU as well as Exhibit “1” of the First 
Amendment to the MOU. 
 
4. 
Incorporation.  This Second Amendment modifies the terms and conditions of the MOU 
and its previous amendments.  Except as modified herein, all other terms and conditions of the 
MOU and the previous amendment to the MOU remain in full force and effect.  This Second 
Amendment is entered into pursuant to paragraph 8A of the MOU pertaining to “Amendments.” 
 
5. 
Signatures.  In witness whereof, the parties to this Amendment through their duly 
authorized representatives have executed this Amendment on the days and dates set out below, 
and certify that they have read, understood, and agreed to the terms and conditions of this 
Amendment as set forth herein. 
 
The effective date of this Amendment is the date of the signature last affixed to this page.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2012-10-22_TotalExtraction_27824483.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Richard Kaysen, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
Sara Vasquez, Asst. Director of Purchasing 
DATE:

the Cheyenne Greenway Walterscheid Underpass project.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2012-10-22_TotalExtraction_23675546.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 October 22, 2012
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne,
Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer,
and Dan White, City Attorney.  Members of Boy Scout Troop 116 led the recitation of the pledge
of allegiance.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)   Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Allen and Mr. Collins agenda item #21(b) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Collins
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA]
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on October 8, 2012.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez each declared a conflict of interest (business interest in vouchers
payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services and Radio Station KYOY FM respectively) and vacated
Council Chambers.  Mr. Pierson moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by
Mr. Spiker.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council
Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez returned to Council
Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation
conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land being situated in a portion of
the East Half of the East Half (E½E½) of Section 20, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th
P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located approximately 150 feet west of the intersection of
Converse Avenue and Ogden Road).  Jim Flesher, Urban Planning Office, reviewed requirements and
compliance of conditions contained in Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402, including specific information
indicating compliance with Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 (a)(i) through (vi).  Ed Murray, Murray Properties,
adjacent land owner and concerned citizen advised he had no problem with the annexation in
principle or the requirements of state statute for compliance, however, he did have concerns with the
proposed road configuration depicted in Exhibit D, Road Network for Section 20, submitted with the
annexation documents.  He stated the proposed configuration contemplates significant deviations
from the master transportation plan and is not in compliance with the long range transportation plans
as outlined in the Unified Development Code (UDC) under Section 4.3.2 (City Clerk’s note: copies

of the UDC section were submitted by Mr. Murray and is on file with the City Clerk’s Office).  Mr.
Murray expressed his concerns with the road configuration and questioned the timing of the submittal
of the transportation network plan, commenting on his belief it should be submitted as part of the
preliminary and/or final plat documents and not with the annexation paperwork.  Kim Rindlisbacher,
Scenic Development, Inc., provided brief comments on the project Scenic is planning on developing
on the property, if annexed.  He advised the project proposed is an upscale apartment community and
stated the project will provide an amenity for the community, noting their development area will be
conducive to the street plans outlined in PlanCheyenne.  Comments followed with regard to having
a master plan for development growth, including roads, of the area in place for review prior to
approval of the annexation request.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the
hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.
ORDINANCE - Creating Section 6.08.180  Feeding Geese and Ducks Prohibited, of Chapter
6.08, Animal Care and Control Regulations Generally, of Title 6, Animals, of the Code of the City
of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash moved to approve on
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the
governing body with the exception of Mr. Pierson and Mr. Valdez voting “no”. (#3962)
[CA]
RESOLUTION - Certifying that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne is in support
of the Town of Albin’s projects to be submitted to the State Loan and Investment Board for the
December 2012 Countywide Consensus Block Grant funding in the total amount of $115,000.
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5436)
[CA]
RESOLUTION - Certifying that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne is in support
of the Town of Pine Bluffs project to be submitted to the State Loan and Investment Board for the
December 2012 Countywide Consensus Block Grant funding in the amount of $26,000.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried. (#5437) 
[CA]
RESOLUTION - Re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2013 budget of the City of Cheyenne
according to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 and 16-4-113.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
(#5438)
[CA]
RESOLUTION - Designating the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority (“DDA”) as
the local Main Street Organization for the implementation of the Wyoming Main Street Program;
authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to join with the DDA in the submission of a Wyoming Main
Street-Certified Track Application to the Wyoming Business Council; and designating the Executive
Director of the DDA as the Program Director for the Wyoming Main Street Program in the City of
Cheyenne.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5439)
RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to enter into an agreement to purchase
537 square feet of land located at the southeast corner of the intersection of East 19th Street and
Converse Avenue, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsor
-- Mr. Brown).  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown advised the item had been discussed
under other items for the agenda at Finance Committee and provisions of Title 2 of City Code had
been met.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City
Attorney, advised the agreement is the result of discussions with the Cole family and would provide
the City with acquisition of land to be affiliated with the City’s roundabout project

(Converse/Pershing/19th  intersection).  He noted it is desired that the transaction proceed at this time
as the Cole family will be closing on their property sale soon involving the Safeway Store site.  Eric
Miller questioned the size of the proposed roundabout, noting he had received an email per his
previous request but would like a response “on the record.”  Mayor Kaysen read a portion of the email
that was sent to Mr. Miller from Nathan Beauheim, Assistant City Engineer, that stated “ ... the
outside diameter of the proposed roundabout is about 230 feet, though this varies a little bit as the
roundabout isn’t a perfect circle...”.  Upon further inquiry, Mr. White stated the land is being
purchased from the Cole Corporation, and Mayor Kaysen and Frank Cole noted that a property
appraisal was done with the land valued at $5,350.00 and that the agreed upon amount for purchase
of the land is $5,000.00.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5440)
[CA]
RESOLUTION - Certifying that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne has agreed on
the Town of Burns substitution project to be submitted to the State Loan and Investment Board for
the 2011-2012 Countywide Consensus Block Grant funding -- referred to Finance Committee
(sponsor -- Mr. Collins).
[CA]
RESOLUTION - Supporting the City of Cheyenne’s application for a Land and Water
Conservation Fund Grant for the Martin Luther King, Jr. Park Quickstart Tennis Court Project --
referred to Finance Committee (sponsors -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Valdez).
[CA]
RESOLUTION - Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to approve and accept the FY2012 Grants
to encourage arrest policies and enforcement of Protection Orders Program from the U.S. Department
of Justice Office on Violence Against Women in the amount of $300,000.00 (Award No. 2012-WE-
AX-0058) -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsor -- Mr. Brown).
[CA]
RESOLUTION - Determining conditions for annexation required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402
exist for a parcel of land being situated in a portion of the East Half of the East Half (E½E½) of
Section 20, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located
approximately 150 feet west of the intersection of Converse Avenue and Ogden Road) -- referred to
Public Services Committee (sponsor -- Ms. Ash).
[CA]
RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne to adopt the
revised statement of investment policy -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsors -- Mr. Collins and
Mr. Brown).
[CA]
Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Transit Division, and Wyoming Department of
Health, Aging Division, to provide supportive services to persons 60 years or older (Title III-B
Program Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5919)    
[CA] 
Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Parks and Recreation Department, and Cheyenne
Capitals Youth Hockey Association, for 2012 - 2013 youth hockey season at the City owned Ice and
Events Center.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5920) 
Ratification of existing Contract No. 5801 between the City of Cheyenne and Associated
Legal Group, LLC, to provide funding for additional legal services as needed for the acquisition of
property for the East Pershing Boulevard Reconstruction Phase IV Project, as provided in Section 6.E.
of said contract.  Mr. Brown reported there was no recommendation on the item from Finance

Committee.  Mr. Brown  moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City
Attorney, explained, pursuant to initiation of the City’s eminent domain action, the existing contract
provides for outside legal counsel who has extensive knowledge and experience in the area of eminent
domain.  He stated there is a clause in the agreement requiring governing body approval if the amount
for services exceeded $35,000.00.  Mr. White advised they are continuing negotiations with the
owners of the Hollywood Video property site and need to continue with the legal services being
provided due to an upcoming hearing and conclusion of the matter, which will exceed the $35,000.00
amount.  Comments followed in support of continuing with the outside legal services, as well as
comment in opposition to taking property generally.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- Ms. Ash, Mr.
Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr.  Allen, Mr.
Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez. (#5801) 
[CA]
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and RBC Global Asset Management (U.S.) Inc. to
provide investment advisory services (Solid Waste, 2007-2010, 2011-2014 1% Sales Tax & 1%
Specific Purpose Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA]
Contract Modification No. 2 to Contract No. 5432 between the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation
Division, and A-1 Organics for resources and services at the public Composting Facility.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried. (#5432)
[CA]
Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 5511 between the City of Cheyenne, Public
Works Department, and TriHydro Corporation for environmental monitoring at the Happy Jack
Landfill.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $209,220.00). (#5511)
[CA]
Second Amendment to Contract #4966 between the City of Cheyenne and Saddle Ridge, LLC,
pertaining to Saddle Ridge Subdivision greenway system improvements -- referred to Finance
Committee. 
[CA]
Consideration of Bid #M-4-13 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Engineering
Department, and Reiman Corporation, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the East Pershing Boulevard
Reconstruction Phase IV Project (1% Sales Tax 2007-2010, State and Local Grants, Water and Sewer
Enterprise funds, Miscellaneous Federal Grants).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not
to exceed $3,651,937.00). (#5921)
Consideration of Bid #M-6-13 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Parks and
Recreation Department, and Simon Contractors, Inc., Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the Cheyenne
Greenway Walterscheid Underpass Project (1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use  Tax, Miscellaneous
Federal Grants).  Mr. Brown moved to accept the bid in an amount not to exceed $785,662.50,
seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Comments followed regarding work previously provided by the contractor
and level of confidence involving “a good product” being received for this project.  Doug Vetter, City
Engineer, explained the bid was the lowest, qualified responsive bidder and provided information on
the Pershing/Snyder project involving the contractor, WYDOT and the City, noting the project is still
unfinished as punch list items have to be corrected prior to finalizing the project.  He advised
WYDOT and the contractor have reached an agreement with regard to some corrections.  Discussion
included the following topics: Previous project concerns relative to quality of services provided by
the contractor; compaction problems; quality of road construction and life of streets; aggregate and
concrete products and problems (i.e. alkali silica reactivity a/k/a ASR); City specifications and

requirements, and on-site inspections during street projects.  During discussion Mr. Vetter provided
information on compaction problems that need to be watched during paving projects; Engineering
Department’s approach of providing provisions for mix designs to best suit project needs; nature of
the underpass project that did not require special provisions being specified; regional problems with
ASR involving aggregate, and concrete mix elements.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Brown,
Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” --  Mr. Allen, Ms.
Ash, Mr. Collins and Dr. Rinne.  Mayor Kaysen requested inspectors monitor the job closely due to
concerns raised during discussion. (#5922)
Authorization to purchase Utility Rocket 5GHZ quadband communication equipment in the
amount of $167,999.72 from CDW- G for the Cheyenne Police Department (SLIB Funding) --
referred to Finance Committee.  Mr. Brown advised the item was discussed under other items for the
agenda at Finance Committee and provisions of Title 2 had been met.  Mr. Brown moved to approve
the purchase from CDW-G of Vernon Hills, IL in an amount not to exceed $167,999.72, seconded
by Mr. Valdez. Captain Mitch Maxwell, Cheyenne Police Department, advised they had recently
received information that a similar product may exist and requested a 2 week postponement to allow
for further evaluation.  Upon inquiry, Captain Maxwell explained the communication equipment
consists of  a box that goes in the trunk of the vehicle and connects to and interfaces with the in-
vehicle computer and camera system, which allows officers to run drivers licenses, etc. in the field.
He explained it will increase the download speed for video transference, boost the cell signal, help
eliminate communication dead zones, and has GPS tracking capability.  Victor Sicone, Buffalo Ridge,
questioned how many “spares” would be provided and how long the equipment would last before
becoming obsolete and has to be replaced.  Mr. Maxwell advised they have allocated funding to equip
all patrol vehicles and spare vehicles and that the average life of the utility rockets is 5-7 years.  Mr.
Brown moved to postpone to Nov. 13, 2012, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Concern was expressed that
Finance Committee members were requested to review the item under other items for the agenda due
to timeliness issues involving installation of vehicle equipment, and staff’s resulting request to
postponement.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. 
Authorization to purchase a used Ice Surface Groomer, 2004 Olympian Model 2000, in the
amount of $55,000.00 from Big Hill Services Ltd., Cochrane, AB, Canada, for the City owned  Ice
and Events Center -- referred to Finance Committee.  Mr. Brown advised the item was discussed
under other items for the agenda at Finance Committee and provisions of Title 2 had been met.  Mr.
Brown moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $55,000.00, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Rick
Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, provided information on recent equipment problems with the
current 1996 ice groomer; problems in acquiring a temporary unit; need for a backup unit to fulfill
contractual obligations to users of the Ice and Events Center for hockey activities; number of daily
times the ice is groomed; other ice centers having 2 to 3 groomers, cost of a new groomer
($100,000.00) and advised purchase of the 2004 model would include a 2 year warranty.  He stated,
if approved, the new unit could be received prior to the next governing body meeting, noting he may
request a counter check be issued due to timing of the delivery, and indicated the current ice groomer
will be kept operational as a backup with regular inspections.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body. 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held November 13, 2012 at 6:00 p.m. in
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a  transfer of ownership for
a retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for Los Abuelos Mexican

Restaurant, LLC d/b/a Los Abuelos Mexican Restaurant, 1601 Central Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.
(License currently held by One East Sixteenth LLC, 1601 Central Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming). 
Announcement was made of the Appointments of Robert Mathia and Melissa Leger to the
Downtown Development Authority Board to be confirmed November 13, 2012.
Announcement was made that due to the Veterans Day Holiday on Monday, November 12,
2012, the regularly scheduled meeting of the Governing Body will instead be held on Tuesday,
November 13, 2012 beginning at 6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers, Municipal Building, 2101
O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.      
Under other business:
 
Barbie Harrington expressed concerns regarding the quality of work done by Simon
Contractors and a recent street project (Pershing/Snyder Ave. area).  She also questioned the source
of funding for the office relocation of the Downtown Development Authority (DDA) and whether the
money the DDA is using for costs involving the move might be unconstitutional pursuant to
provisions of the Wyoming Constitution.  Mayor Kaysen advised the funding for the DDA move is
a loan through a joint powers board and not directly from the City.  It was noted that the joint powers
board involved was scheduled to meet the next day (Oct. 23 ) at 7:00 a.m. at the Align Building off
rd
of Airport Parkway. 
Eric Miller commented on the quality of concrete pours for the Pershing/Snyder project, as
previously referenced by Ms. Harrington, and suggested core tests be done with inspections for these
types of projects.
Brad Harrington advised he had done research on the United Nation’s Agenda 21 document
and the City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) and had information regarding this that he would
like to present to the governing body, noting it would take him some time to review general Agenda
21 information as well as facts and details.  Upon inquiry, he explained the purpose of providing the
information is to demonstrate that codes of the City are connected with Agenda 21.  Mayor Kaysen
suggested Mr. Harrington distribute the information packets he had prepared to members of the
governing body to give them an opportunity to review the research.  Mayor Kaysen stated if Mr.
Harrington so desired he would make himself available for further discussion on the topic.  Mr.
Harrington submitted copies of the information packets (titled “On Agenda 21: The United Nations’
Blueprint for Collectivization and Enslavement, And on How It’s Being Implemented on a Local
Level Right Here in Cheyenne, Wyoming”) to governing body members (document on file with City
Clerk’s Office).  Comment was made that perhaps the topic could be reviewed at a public work
session.  Mayor Kaysen noted that the UDC is undergoing its first annual review for presentation to
the governing body for any changes and, as part of the review process, there will be the opportunity
for public involvement.

There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 7:17 p.m.
Submitted by,
Carol Intlekofer, MMC
City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published:
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
October 30, 2012


--- END OF FILE ---

N00°01'57”W A Distance Of 5293.10 Feet More Or Less To The Point Of 
Beginning. 
 
Said parcels of land contain 1,252.09 acres, more or less. 
 
(b) 
That an accurate annexation map depicting the area to be annexed by this ordinance 
is attached to this ordinance as Exhibit A, said map prepared by Adam E. Deschler, 
a Professional Land Surveyor, licensed in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 
12045; 
 
(c) 
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(d) 
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(e) 
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and other 
services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, be 
furnished; 
 
(f) 
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned LU – Land 
Use by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 
 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and its 
governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the entire annexed 
area. 
 
Section 2. That the City of Cheyenne hereby further finds as follows: 
 
(a) 
That the City of Cheyenne is the sole owner in fee of the below-described real 
property; that such property is not contiguous to the existing corporate limits of the 
City; and that the City of Cheyenne hereby elects to annex such property and to 
make it a part of the City pursuant to W.S. 15-1-407. The property to be annexed is 
described as follows: 
 
A Parcel Of Land Situated In A Portion Of The South One-Half Of Section 31, 
Township 13 North, Range 67 West Of The 6th Principal Meridian, Laramie 
County, Wyoming, Said Parcel Described In Book 492 Page 70 Of The Laramie 
County Records, more particularly described as follows: 
 
Commencing At The South One-Quarter Corner Of Said Section 31, 
Thence N15°05'00”W A Distance Of 596.64 Feet To A Point On The Parcel 
Described In Book 492, Page 70 And The Point Of Beginning; Thence 
N00°18'19”E A Distance Of 575.00 Feet; Thence S89°41'41”E A Distance 
Of 775.00 Feet; Thence S00°18'19”W A Distance Of 575.00 Feet; Thence 

N89°41'41”W A Distance Of 775.00 Feet More Or Less To The Point Of 
Beginning. 
 
Said parcel of land contains 10.23 acres, more or less. 
 
(b) 
That, pursuant to W.S. 15-1-407, the City of Cheyenne may annex non-contiguous 
land owned solely by the City by ordinance and the annexation of the above-
described property is undertaken in accordance with such authority. 
 
Section 3.  That all of the real property described in Sections 1 and 2 and depicted on 
Exhibit A is hereby annexed to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof. 
 
Section 4.  That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance, together with the map identified as Exhibit A, with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio 
Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office 
to add said area to the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 5.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared illegal 
or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining 
phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 6.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
PUDC-26-37: Cox Lands - Annexation 
REQUEST: 
An annexation of 1,259.91 acres of land. 
LOCATION: 
West of Roundtop Road and adjacent to Happy Jack Road. 
APPLICANT: 
Cox, William S Revocable Trust and the City of Cheyenne 
AGENT: 
AVI P.C 
OWNERS: 
Cox, William S Revocable Trust and City of Cheyenne 
PREPARED BY: 
 Connor White, Planner II 
 
 

PUDC-26-37 
 
 
April 16, 2026 
2 | P a g e 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicants are requesting annexing roughly 1,259.91 acres of land to allow for industrial development. The 
Cox Ranch land is currently vacant. The City owned land has a water treatment facility operating on it. The 
annexed area will be assigned a City zone based on the underlying County zone. The applicant for the Cox 
Ranch Land has submitted a Future Land Use Map amendment (PUDC-26-48) and a Zoning Map Amendment 
(PUDC-26-38).  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by an assigned 
City zone of AG Agricultural for the Cox Ranch Land and P Public for the City owed land.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of 
the persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the future 
occupants. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to City Fire and Police and 
sanitation services. By being within City Limits the property will be able to connect to City services.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.

PUDC-26-37 
 
 
April 16, 2026 
3 | P a g e 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 
15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
The Cox Ranch Land is currently vacant while the City owned parcels are developed with a water 
treatment facility. They are surrounded by vacant land on most sides with the Cox Ranch Land 
being adjacent to City limits along the southeast edge. With the annexation of the Cox Ranch 
Land it would make the City owned parcel along Happy Jack contiguous, but the other parcel the 
City owns will still not be contiguous. The annexation of these properties will benefit those who 
live within the City and will be a part of the social and economic fabric that is Cheyenne. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing 
city or town and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to 
residents of the city or town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed 
to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection will 
be extended upon annexation. Utilities are within the area.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city 
or town, or the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iv)] 
 
The Cox Ranch Land is roughly 5.26% contiguous. This seemingly small % is due to the size of the 
annexation. The applicant could annex approximately 194ac with 15% contiguity, and two 
annexations each with 15% contiguity could result in annexing the entire area. The parcel the City 
Owned parcel does not become contiguous without the annexation of the Cox Ranch Land and a 
portion of the City owned land is not an will not be contiguous. Because the land is owned by the 
City, it does not need to be contiguous when annexing.    
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing 
body is prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the 
annexed area pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
 

PUDC-26-37 
 
 
April 16, 2026 
4 | P a g e 
 
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the 
public hearing required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all 
landowners and affected public utilities within the territory a summary of the 
proposed annexation report as required under subsection (c) of this section and 
notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing required by W.S. 15-1-
405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on March 13, 2026. Property 
owners within 300-feet of the area to be annexed were also mailed notice at this time. The public 
hearing for this annexation is scheduled for April 13, 2026. Twenty (20) business days (on the 
City’s calendar) prior to the announced public hearing is March 16, 2026. The earliest confirmation 
of delivery was dated March 16, 2026. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with Commercial uses. These areas are expected to develop with full access to 
urban services – such areas are best served by being within City limits. The area is within the Urban Service 
Boundary. 
 
PUBLIC NOTICE AND PUBLIC COMMENTS RECEIVED: 
Although projects have public notice and the opportunity for public comments, projects are required to 
be approved or denied based on the review criteria (listed above). Public comments relevant to the review 
criteria may assist the review and discussion on the application. 
  
The public was given notice of the project in the following ways: 
- 
Legal Notice in the Wyoming Tribune Eagle published on March 21, 2026 and April 4, 2026. 
- 
Mailed Notice was sent out by Staff on March 13, 2026 to all property owners within the 
annexation and within 300 feet of the area to be annexed.  
- 
Posted Notice Evidence of posted notice was provided that signs were posted on March 30, 
2026.  
- 
Internet Posting on connect.cheyennecity.org on February 16, 2026. 
 
As of the publication of the Staff Report (April 8, 2026), Staff has received one inquiry from the public on 
this project. The inquiry was about what may be planned for the Cox Ranch Land. The project page on the 
website has received 92 views and no comments. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
No major comments were received.  
 
 

PUDC-26-37 
 
 
April 16, 2026 
5 | P a g e 
 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of the 
annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
services typically available to urban areas are available to be extended to this property.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Submitted Application 
4. Applicant-submitted annexation map
